1.      Please provide an executive summary describing the nature and scope of the proposal. Additionally,
please include the following: (1) identification of all parties, (2) description of the applicant and it’s
licensure track record, (3) the type of transaction proposed including description of the transaction and
relevant costs, (4) summary of all transfer documents, (5) summary of the organizational structure of the
applicant and its affiliates, and (6) whether the facility will be accredited.

CBC Rhode Island FT, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company (the “Applicant”) is requesting
Change in Effective Control (“CEC”) approval of the planned acquisition of all of the issued and
outstanding membership interests in Hope Nursing Home Care, LLC, a Rhode Island limited
liability company (“Hope Nursing” or “Facility”) held by Irene Qi (“Seller”). Hope Nursing is a
licensed Home Nursing Care provider with a main office located at 1049 Park Avenue, Cranston,
Rhode Island 02910. Since the planned acquisition is of the equity interests of Hope Nursing
(see Q14 response), the licensee will remain Hope Nursing and will continue to operate as a licensed
and Medicare certified home nursing care provider. As is explained in further detail below, Hope
Nursing will become part of the Community Based Care family of home health care providers.
Community Based Care has a main office located in Raleigh, North Carolina and operates home
based care providers in North Carolina, Virginia and Florida. See: https://cbcare.com/. The
Community Based Care operations are managed by a seasoned management team let by Michael
Kotzen as President and CEO. Mr. Kotzen is responsible for developing and implementing the
overall mission of Community Based Care and ensuring the delivery of the highest quality service
to its clients and families. Mr. Kotzen has more than twenty-five years of diverse healthcare
leadership experience. He earned a Bachelor’s degree in Business Administration from Rutgers
University and a MBA from St. Joseph’s University. Mr. Kotzen is a fellow in the American College
of Healthcare Executives.
Following the approval of this CEC Application, pursuant to the terms of a Membership Interest
Purchase Agreement dated September 9, 2021, the Applicant plans to acquire 100 % of the
membership interest in Hope Nursing for an aggregate purchase price of $18,589,000
(the “Acquisition”).
The Applicant is a newly formed entity that was formed for purposes of the Acquisition and 100%
of the membership interests of the Applicant are held by Community Based Care FT Acquisition,
LLC, which in turn is 100% owned by Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP. Community
Based Care Intermediate I, LP is 99% owned by Community Based Care Holdings, LP and 1%
owned by Martis Partners III Community Aggregator, LP. Community Based Care Holdings, LP is
owned 4.90% collectively by individuals and 95.10% by Martis Partners III Community
Aggregator, LP. Martis Partners III Community Aggregator, L.P. is 20.09% owned by Martis
Partners III Community Blocker, LP and 79.91% owned by Martis Partners III, LP. Martis
Partners III Community Aggregator is managed by Martis Partners III GP, LLC.
Martis Partners III Community Blocker, LP and Martis Partners III, LP are owned by passive
investors that commit capital to Martis but do not have involvement in or control of investment
decisions made by Martis or the day to day operations of Applicant. Community Based Care
Intermediate I, LP also owns 100% of the stock of Community Based Care Acquisition, Inc., which
owns 100% of the membership interest of Community Based Care, LLC, Community Based Care,
LLC in turn owns interests in several home health providers, and is currently operating in 3
states. See Community Based Care Structure in response to Question 15. See Tab 1, attached to
demonstrate that the passive investors do not have involvement in or control of investment
decisions made by Martis or the day-to-day operations of the Applicant.
While the Applicant was formed for purposes of the Acquisition, the Facility will be operated as
part of the Community Based Care family of health care providers, all under common upstream
ownership. As such, where appropriate, the application presents information relative to the
historic and current Community Based Care operations.
The Facility will be fully accredited.

11.     Please provide a concise description of the services currently offered by the licensed entity and
identify any services that will be added, terminated, expanded, or reduced and state the reasons therefore:
Hope Nursing Home Care provides home nursing care services to Rhode Island patients who require
assistance with everyday living, including health education and supportive services to those in need.
Hope Nursing Home Care services include personal care, homemaking and companion care.
The Applicant intends to continue with the mission established by Hope Nursing Home Care to
provide patients home nursing care services to support patients and their families. The Applicant
does not anticipate that any services will be added, terminated, expanded or reduced from those
currently offered and intends to provide services within the scope of its license.

12.     Please identify the long-term plans of the applicant with respect to the health care programs and
health care services to be provided at the facility:
The Applicant does not currently anticipate altering the services provided and plans to continue to
provide quality, patient centric home health nursing services to its patients.

As noted in response to Question 1 and as set forth in Tab 15 Organization Charts, the Applicant
provides services in several states, including services to traditionally underserved populations,
including Medicaid and charity care services under applicable regulations and policies. The
Applicant believes that it has a strong track record with respect to providing access to traditionally
underserved populations in its service areas.
17.     Please identify the proposed immediate and long-term plans of the applicant to ensure adequate and
appropriate access to the program and health care services to be provided by the health care facility/ies to
traditionally underserved populations.

The Applicant plans to accept all patients regardless of their insurance coverage or ability to
pay. The Applicant will also comply with all applicable policies and procedures required for
Medicare certification and to Medicare and Medicaid patients.

and their respective affiliates.
          A. Citations, enforcement actions, violations, charges, investigations, or similar types of actions
             involving the applicant and/or its affiliates (including but not limited to actions brought
             forward by any governmental agency, accrediting agency, or similar type of an agency.);

             any court of law, in which the applicant and/or its affiliates and/or any officers, directors,

                        Residential), State of North Carolina, Wake County, General Court of Justice,
                        Superior Court Division, Court File No. 20 CVS 8016).

                 This is a personal injury case against Community Innovations, a Community Based
                 Care subsidiary. Community Innovations filed a motion to dismiss which was denied.
                 The matter is now proceeding to discovery.

                 Date of filing: 2020
                 Status: Pending

              2.     EEOC v. Community Innovations, Inc., U.S. District Court for the
                     Eastern District of North Carolina (Western Division), Civil Action
                     No. 5:17-CV-00599.

              This was a charge of retaliation under Title VII of the Civil Rights Act. Community
              Innovations (a Community Based Care subsidiary) denied liability but settled the
              matter to avoid further litigation expense.

              Date of filing: December 5, 2017
              Status: Settled - March 20, 2019

              3.     Aym Technologies, LLC v. Gene Rodgers, Scopia Capital Management LLP and
                     Community Based Care, LLC, No. 16 CVS 21788 (N.C. Business Court)

              Aym was a business dispute between Aym Technologies and the previous owner of
              Community Based Care. Community Based Care was named as a defendant but was
              dismissed. Summary judgment on behalf of all defendants was granted.

              Date of filing: December 5, 2017
              Status: Settled - August 2021; dismissal filed Sep. 22, 2021

              4.     Scopia Capital Management LP, Homecare Management Corporation and
                     Community Based Care LLC v. Lewis Quinn and Aym Technologies, LLC, Index
                     No. 152069/2017 (Supreme Court of the State of New York, New York
                     County)

              Scopia v. Lewis Quinn was a companion case to Aym filed in New York court. The
              case was settled and has been fully resolved.

              Date of filing: 2017 (date unknown)
              Status: Settled - August 2021

              5.     Michael Sunderman v. Deborah Louise Richards and Lindley Habilitation
                     Services, Inc. (Florida)

              This is a personal injury car accident case. This is an insured claim.

              6.     Richard Rummell v. Serenity Village, Inc., and Suncoast New Options, Inc.,
                     Case No. 21-001314-CI (6th Judicial Cir. Pinellas County, FL) (filed June 30,
                     2021).

              This is a personal injury case. Suncoast New Options, Inc., a subsidiary of
              Community Based Care, is still investigating this matter. This is an insured claim.

21.   Please identify any planned actions of the applicant to reduce, limit, or contain health care costs
and improve the efficiency with which health care services are delivered to the citizens of this state.

Using its patient centric local approach to care that is supported by the resources and experience
of the larger Community Based Care organization, the Applicant plans to improve efficiency of
care in a cost effective manner. The Applicant believes that it has the experience to effectively
manage costs without adversely affecting care delivery and is well positioned to respond to
rapidly evolving health care reform efforts.
22.    Please provide a copy of the Quality Assurance Policies (for the services) and a detailed
explanation of how quality assurance for patient services will be implemented at the facility/ies by the
applicant.

See Tab 22, attached for copies of the Licensee’s Quality Assurance related policies.                   The
Applicant intends to continue operating the Licensee in accordance with such policies.

23.   Please provide a detailed description about the amount and source of the equity and debt
commitment for this transaction. (NOTE: If debt is contemplated as part of the financing, please complete
Appendix E). Additionally, please demonstrate the following:
        A. The immediate and long-term financial feasibility of the proposed financing plan;

              Hope Nursing Home Care has operated as a profitable, stable home and health care
              provider for many years and the Applicant expects operational results to remain positive
              following the acquisition. As set forth in Appendix A, the Facility will have adequate
              resources to meet capital and operating needs. Further, the Applicant will be operated
              as a Community Based Care entity and Community Based Care successfully operates
              home care and related healthcare provider entities in other states and has the financial
              resources and capabilities to support the Facility’s financial needs in the event of any
              operational deficiencies at the Facility.
        B. The relative availability of funds for capital and operating needs; and

              As noted in Appendix A, the Facility is projected to have sufficient funds for capital and
              operating needs of the Facility.
        C. The applicant’s financial capability.

              The financial statements of Community Based Care in Tab 28, attached, demonstrate the
              Applicant’s financial capability.

OFFICER’S CERTIFICATE

January 19, 2022

The undersigned, being a duly appointed, qualified and acting officer of the general partner
(the “General Partner”) of each of Martis Partners III Community Aggregator, L.P., a Delaware
limited partnership, Martis Partners III, L.P., a Delaware limited partnership, and Martis Partners
Ill PV, L.P., a Delaware limited partnership, does hereby certify on behalf of the General Partner,
and not in any individual capacity, that he is authorized to execute and deliver this Officer’s
Certificate (this “Certificate”), dated as of the date hereof.

The undersigned hereby further certifies as follows:

1. Owen Davis is a duly appointed, qualified, and acting Manager Member, and, in his
capacity as a Manager Member, is duly authorized to act on behalf of the General
Partner.

2. No individual investors in Martis Partners III, L.P. or Martis Partners II] PV, L.P. have
(a) involvement in the investment decisions made by General Partner on behalf of Martis
Partners III, L.P. or Martis Partners III] PV, L.P. or (b) involvement in or control of the
day to day operations of CBC Rhode Island FT, LLC.

                 MEMBERSHIP INTEREST PURCHASE AGREEMENT

       This Membership Interest Purchase Agreement(this "Agreement"), dated as of September
9, 2021 (the "Effective Date"), is entered into by and among Community Based Care, LLC, a
Delaware limited liability company ("Buyer"), Hope Nursing Home Care, LLC, a Rhode Island
limited liability company (the "Company"), and Irene Aiyun Qi, an individual resident of
Massachusetts ("Seller"). Buyer, Seller, and the Company are sometimes referred to herein as a
"Party" or collectively as the "Parties."

                                          RECITALS

        WHEREAS, Seller collectively owns all of the issued and outstanding membership
interests of the Company as of the date hereof(collectively the "Units");

        WHEREAS, Seller wishes to sell to Buyer, and Buyer wishes to purchase from Seller, the
Units, subject to the terms and conditions set forth herein;

         WHEREAS, a portion of the purchase price payable by Buyer to Seller shall be placed in
escrow by Buyer, the release of which shall be contingent upon certain events and conditions, all
as set forth in this Agreement and the Escrow Agreement(as defined herein);

        WHEREAS, Seller will substantially benefit from the consummation of the transactions
contemplated by this Agreement and, following the closing of the transactions contemplated by
this Agreement, the going concern of the Company would be diminished substantially if Seller
were to breach their obligations under this Agreement in any material way,including the covenants
set forth in Article VI hereof; and

        WHEREAS,as a result of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, the Company
will become a wholly owned subsidiary of Buyer, and Buyer will own one hundred percent(100%)
of the membership interests of the Company.

        NOW,THEREFORE,in consideration of the mutual covenants and agreements hereinafter
set forth and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are
hereby acknowledged, the Parties agree as follows:

                                          ARTICLE II
                                       PURCHASE AND SALE

        Section 2.01 Purchase and Sale. Subject to the terms and conditions set forth herein
and on the basis of the representations and warranties hereinafter set forth, at the Closing, Seller
shall sell, transfer, convey, assign and deliver to Buyer, and Buyer shall purchase from Seller, the
Units, free and clear of all Encumbrances(other than restrictions on transfer imposed by applicable
federal and state securities laws), for the consideration specified in Section 2.02.

        Section 2.02 Purchase Price; Closing Cash Payment. The aggregate purchase price
(the "Purchase   Price") to be paid by Buyer to Seller for the Units, subject to adjustment pursuant
 to Section 2.05 hereof, shall be payable as follows:
       (a)      $18,589,000 (the "Stated Cash Payment");
       (b)      the "Closing Cash Payment" shall be the sum of:

               (i)     the Stated Cash Payment;
               (ii)    minus the amount of the Indemnity Escrow Fund, including one-half of the
fee for the Indemnity Escrow Fund, payable pursuant to Section 2.03;
               (iii)   minus the amount of any unpaid Company Transaction Expenses as set
forth in the Closing Transaction Expenses Certificate;

                                                  11

===== PAGE 102 =====
               (iv)    minus the amount of any Closing Debt;
               (v)     minus the amount of any unpaid Change of Control Payments;
               (vi)    plus the amount of the Estimated Closing Cash on Hand Surplus (if any);
               (vii)   minus the amount of the Estimated Closing Cash on Hand Shortfall (if any).
      (c)     Tax Election. If Buyer opts to cause the Company and the Seller to make the
Section 336(e)Election, Buyer shall promptly notify the Seller ofits election. On the Closing Date,
the Company and Seller shall deliver executed versions of the Section 336(e) Election Agreement
and the Section 336(e) Election Statement. The obligations of Buyer and Seller set forth in this
Section 2.02(c) shall survive the Closing.

         Section 2.03 Indemnity Escrow Fund. At the Closing, (a) an amount equal to
$1,858,900.00 (together with all earnings thereon, the "Indemnity Escrow Fund") shall be
deposited by wire transfer of immediately available funds with Escrow Agent, for purposes of
satisfying any of Seller's obligations pursuant to Article VII or Article IX herein, as well as to
cover any unpaid Accrued Expenses. Any fees for the Indemnity Escrow Fund shall be borne by
in equal portions by Seller and Buyer and deposited with Escrow Agent at the Closing. To the
 extent that fees for the Indemnity Escrow Fund are deducted from the Indemnity Escrow Fund
 after the Closing, Buyer shall reimburse the Indemnity Escrow Fund or Seller for Buyer's one-half
share of said fees. The Indemnity Escrow Fund shall be held, invested, and disbursed by Escrow
 Agent as specified in and pursuant to the terms and conditions of an escrow agreement, to be
 negotiated by the parties and the Escrow Agent as to usual and customary terms prior to Closing
(the "Escrow Agreement"), and in accordance with the terms and conditions of Article VII and
 Article IX hereof, and as necessary to serve as cash on hand for Buyer to cover any unpaid Accrued
Expenses for the period of three (3) months following the Closing Date as provided in Article IX
 herein. Should any unpaid Accrued Expenses remain after three (3) months following the Closing
Date, such expenses shall become a claim against the Indemnity Escrow Fund as provided in
 Article IX herein. Buyer and Seller acknowledge and agree that the Indemnity Escrow Fund
 remaining on the date that is twelve(12) months following the Closing Date (the "Escrow Release
Date") shall automatically be disbursed by Escrow Agent to Seller on the fifth (5) Business Day
 after the Escrow Release Date unless Buyer, prior to 5:00 p.m. Eastern Time on the Escrow Release
 Date, provides Escrow Agent with notice that Buyer reasonably believes all or a portion of the
 remaining balance needs to be retained to pay any known or reasonably expected Third Party
 Claims or Direct Claims. In the event that Buyer provides such notice, then Seller shall have thirty
(30) days after Seller's receipt of such notice to notify Buyer in writing that it disagrees with
 Buyer's assertion that a portion of the funds need to be retained in order to pay any known or
 reasonably expected Third Party Claims or Direct Claims. Upon receipt of such objections, Buyer
 and Seller shall attempt to resolve such issue using the procedure set forth in Section 9.04. In the
 event there are unresolved Third Party Claims or Direct Claims and Escrow Agent retains a portion
 of the Indemnity Escrow Fund beyond the Escrow Release Date, Escrow Agent shall disburse the
 balance of the Indemnity Escrow Fund only upon joint written instruction by Buyer and Seller.

        Section 2.05 Purchase Price Adjustment.
        (a)     Adjustments for Tax Purposes. Any payments made pursuant to this Section 2.05
 shall be treated as an adjustment to the Purchase Price by the Parties for Tax purposes, unless
 otherwise required by Law.
        (b)     Closing Cash on Hand Adjustment.

                (i)     Closing Adjustment. At least three (3) Business Days prior to the Closing,
 Seller shall prepare and deliver to Buyer a statement setting forth its good faith estimate of the
 Cash on Hand at the Closing (the "Estimated Closing Cash on Hand"). The Closing Cash
 Payment shall account for any Estimated Closing Cash on Hand Surplus or Estimated Closing
 Cash on Hand Shortfall as set forth in Section 2.02(b).
              (ii)     Post-Closing Adjustment. Within sixty (60) days following the Closing,
 Buyer shall prepare and deliver to Seller a statement setting forth its final determination of the
 Cash on Hand at the Closing (the "Closing Cash on Hand"). Such calculation shall be promptly
 reviewed by Seller, and if not objected to within thirty (30) days of delivery to Seller by Buyer,
 shall be deemed to have been accepted by Seller. Upon acceptance of such statement by Seller,
 any excess of the Closing Cash on Hand over the Estimated Closing Cash on Hand shall be paid

                                                   13

===== PAGE 104 =====
by Buyer to Seller in immediately available funds within five (5) Business Days, and any shortfall
of the Closing Cash on Hand under the Estimated Closing Cash on Hand shall be paid by Seller to
Buyer in immediately available funds within five (5) Business Days, as applicable. The payment
obligations set forth in this Section 2.05(b)(ii) shall survive the Closing.
      (c)      Gross-Up Payment.

               (i)      If Buyer opts to cause the Seller and the Company to make a Section 336(e)
Election, Buyer  shall pay  to Seller an additional amount(the "336 Gross-Up Payment")necessary
to cause Seller's after-tax net proceeds from the sale of the Shares with the 336(e) Election to be
equal to the after-tax net proceeds that Seller would have received had the 336(e)Election not been
made; provided that the 336 Gross-Up Payment shall be calculated in accordance with the
following principles and assumptions:
                     (A)     The 336 Gross-Up Payment shall be calculated as if the Company
                              was an accrual method taxpayer and shall not include any
                              incremental amounts that the Company or Seller may be required
                              to recognize as income or gain as a result of using a cash method
                              of accounting for tax purposes on or prior to the Closing Date
                             (including with respect to any accounts receivable deemed to be
                              sold in connection with the transactions contemplated by this
                              Agreement);
                     (B)     The 336 Gross-Up Payment shall be based upon(x) the effective
                             federal, state and local rates applicable to the Company, taking into
                             account the deductibility of state and local Taxes for U.S. federal
                             income tax purposes and shall assume that the income, gain or loss
                             from the transactions contemplated by this Agreement are the
                             Seller's only income, gain or loss for the applicable Tax year and
                             (y) the Company's tax basis in the assets of the company and the
                              Seller's tax basis in the Company; and
                      (C)     The 336 Gross-Up Payment shall be calculated in a manner
                              consistent with the Allocation Schedule.
               (ii)    If the Buyer opts to cause the Seller and the Company to make a Section
336(e)Election, Buyer shall promptly prepare and deliver to Seller a statement, calculated in good
faith and taking into account the principles and assumptions set forth above, setting forth its
calculation of the 336 Gross-Up Payment. Seller shall provide comments to the Buyer's calculation
of the 336 Gross-Up Payment within thirty (30) days after receipt and Buyer and Seller shall
cooperate in finalizing the calculation of the 336 Gross-Up Payment. Once such 336 Gross-Up
Payment has been finalized, the Buyer shall promptly, and in any event within five (5) days, pay
such amount to the Seller. The Buyer and Seller agree to treat the 336 Gross-Up Payment as
additional purchase price consideration for federal, state, and local income and capital gains tax
purposes. Buyer's payment obligations set forth in this Section 2.05(c) shall survive the Closing.

        Section 2.06 Closing. Subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement, the
purchase and sale of the Units contemplated hereby shall take place at a closing (the "Closing")
no later than two (2) Business Days after the last of the conditions to Closing set forth in Article
 VIII have been satisfied to Buyer's sole satisfaction or waived in writing (other than conditions
 which, by their nature, are to be satisfied on the Closing Date), at the offices of Smith Anderson
Blount Dorsett Mitchell & Jernigan, L.L.P., 150 Fayetteville Street, Suite 2300, Raleigh, North
Carolina, 27601, or at such other time or on such other date or at such other place as Seller and
Buyer may mutually agree upon in writing (the day on which the Closing takes place being the
"Closing Date"). The Closing shall be effective at 11:59 p.m. Eastern Time on the Closing Date
(the "Closing Time").

        Section 2.07 Withholding Tax. Buyer and the Company shall be entitled to deduct and
withhold from the Purchase Price all Taxes that Buyer and the Company may be required to deduct
and withhold under any provision of Tax Law;provided, however, that Buyer shall provide Seller
with written notice of any such withholding obligation at least ten (10) days prior to such
withholding and shall provide reasonable cooperation with Seller to reduce or eliminate such
withholding obligation in accordance with applicable Law. All such withheld amounts shall be
treated as delivered to Seller hereunder.

         Section 2.08 Accounts Receivable. The collections from accounts receivable of the
Company that arise from services rendered prior to the Closing Time and outstanding as of the
 Closing Time(the "Accounts Receivable")shall be owned by Seller(which shall utilize Company
 as its agent for such collection). All amounts collected by Buyer or Company as payments for
 Accounts Receivable shall be promptly and regularly disbursed to Seller(in any event within thirty
(30) days of collection), except according to the provisions of Section 9.04 or in the case that there
 are any unpaid Accrued Expenses (as defined below), in which case Buyer shall be allowed to
 retain such payments to offset such unpaid Accrued Expenses. In the event that the payer has not
 provided adequate instructions on the face of the remittance or any accompanying documentation
 or correspondence to identify the account or invoice for which payment is made, Buyer shall
 undertake reasonable efforts to contact the payer to ascertain how the payment is to be applied. In
 the event that such inquiry is unsuccessful, then such payments shall be applied first to the oldest
 outstanding invoice(s) or accounts receivable for such payer. Buyer shall undertake such
 accounting for the benefit of Seller but shall have sole authority for collection and accounting
 under this Section 2.08. Buyer shall use commercially reasonable efforts to collect the Accounts
 Receivable. To the extent that any of the Accounts Receivable have not been collected within one
 hundred (100) days following the Closing, Buyer shall, upon Seller's written request, assign such
 Accounts Receivable as Seller may designate in writing to Seller, and Seller may be entitled to use
 its commercially reasonable efforts to collect on such Accounts Receivable; provided, that such
 collection activities shall be conducted solely in coordination with and under the supervision of an
 authorized representative of the Company; provided further that neither Seller nor any
 Representatives of Seller will contact any customer of the Company without Buyer's specific prior
 approval.


                                                  15

===== PAGE 106 =====
       Section 2.09 Accrued Expenses. All expenses accrued and payable on or before the
Closing Date and all expenses partially accrued before the Closing Date and payable after the
Closing Date (the "Accrued Expenses") shall be liabilities of Seller and, as a result, shall reduce
the payments otherwise due to Seller under Section 2.08 and Section 9.04. Expenses that are
accrued both before and after the Closing Date shall be pro-rated and charged proportionately
between Buyer and Seller. Further, during the three (3) month period following the Closing Date,
should Seller receive any payments from third parties for Accrued Expenses, Seller shall remit
such payments to Buyer to allow Buyer to pay such expenses. For the avoidance of doubt, such
expenses shall include wages and related taxes and withholdings (including federal and state
withholding taxes, FICA taxes, FUTA premiums, and Medicare taxes) accrued on or before the
Closing Date and outstanding lease payments.

Company is a limited liability company duly organized, validly existing, and in good standing
under the Laws of the State of Rhode Island and has full limited liability company power and
authority to own, operate, or lease the properties and assets now owned, operated, or leased by it
and to carry on its business as it has been and is currently conducted. The Company is only licensed
or qualified to do business in the State of Rhode Island and is in good standing in such jurisdiction.
The Company is not required to be licensed or qualified to do business in any other jurisdiction.
The copies of the Company's Governing Documents which have been furnished to Buyer reflect
all amendments made thereto at any time prior to the date of this Agreement and are correct and
complete in all material respects. The Company is not in default in any respect under or in violation
of any provision of its Governing Documents. All actions taken by the Company in connection
with this Agreement and the other Transaction Documents will be duly authorized'on or prior to
the Closing.

paid, and non-assessable, and are owned of record and beneficially by Seller, free and clear of all
Encumbrances (except for restrictions on transfer imposed by applicable federal and state
securities laws). Seller has all requisite approvals and authorities necessary to transfer the Units.

       (d)     cause the Company to continue in full force and effect without modification all
Insurance Policies, except as required by applicable Law;
      (e)     cause the Company to defend and protect its properties and assets from
infringement or usurpation;

       (0       cause the Company to perform all of its obligations in all material respects under
all Contracts relating to or affecting its properties, assets, or business;

       (g)      cause the Company to maintain its books and records in accordance with past
practice;
       (h)      cause the Company to comply in all material respects with all applicable Laws; and
      (i)     cause the Company not to take or permit any action that would cause any of the
changes, events, or conditions described in Section 3.07 to occur.

        Section 6.02 Access to Information. From the Effective Date until the Closing or earlier
termination of this Agreement, Seller shall, and shall cause the Company to,(a) afford Buyer and
its Representatives full and free access to and the right to inspect all of the Real Property,
properties, assets, premises, books and records, Contracts, and other documents and data related
to the Company;(b)furnish Buyer and its Representatives with such financial, operating, and other
data and information related to the Company as Buyer or any ofits Representatives may reasonably
request subject to compliance with applicable Law; and (c) instruct the Representatives of Seller
and the Company to cooperate with Buyer in its investigation of the Company. Any investigation
pursuant to this Section 6.02 shall be conducted in such manner as not to interfere unreasonably
with the conduct of the business of the Company. No investigation by Buyer or other information
received by Buyer shall operate as a waiver or otherwise affect any representation, warranty, or
agreement given or made by Seller in this Agreement.

        Section 6.03 No Solicitation of Other Bids.
        (a)      From the time of execution of the letter of intent from Buyer to Seller dated June
8, 2021, Seller has not, and has not authorized or permitted any of its Affiliates (including the
Company) or any of its or their Representatives to directly or indirectly (i) encourage, solicit,
initiate, facilitate, or continue- inquiries regarding an Acquisition Proposal; (ii) enter into
discussions or negotiations with, or provide any information to, any Person concerning a possible
Acquisition Proposal; or (iii) enter into any agreements or other instruments (whether or not
binding) regarding an Acquisition Proposal.
        (b)      From the Effective Date until the Closing or earlier termination of this Agreement,
Seller shall not, and shall not authorize or permit any of its Affiliates (including the Company) or
any of its or their Representatives to, directly or indirectly,(i) encourage, solicit, initiate, facilitate,
or continue inquiries regarding an Acquisition Proposal; (ii) enter into discussions or negotiations
 with, or provide any information to, any Person concerning a possible Acquisition Proposal; or
(iii) enter into any agreements or other instruments (whether or not binding) regarding an
 Acquisition Proposal. Seller shall immediately cease and cause to be terminated, and shall cause

                                                    42

===== PAGE 133 =====
its Affiliates (including the Company)and all of its and their Representatives to immediately cease
and cause to be terminated, all existing discussions or negotiations with any Persons conducted
heretofore with respect to, or that could lead to, an Acquisition Proposal. For purposes hereof,
"Acquisition Proposal" shall mean any inquiry, proposal, or offer from any Person (other than
Buyer or any of its Affiliates) concerning(A)a merger, consolidation, liquidation, recapitalization,
equity interest exchange, or other business combination transaction involving the Company;(B)
the issuance or acquisition of units of membership interest or other equity securities of the
Company; or(C) the sale, lease, exchange, or other disposition of any significant portion of the
Company's properties or assets.

       (c)      From the Effective Date until the Closing or earlier termination of this Agreement,
in addition to the other obligations under this Section 6.03, Seller shall promptly(and in any event
within three (3) Business Days after receipt thereof by Seller or its Representatives) advise Buyer
orally and in writing of any Acquisition Proposal, any request for information with respect to any
Acquisition Proposal, or any inquiry with respect to or which could reasonably be expected to
result in an Acquisition Proposal, the material terms and conditions of such request, Acquisition
Proposal or inquiry, and the identity of the Person making the same.
       (d)     Seller agrees that the rights and remedies for noncompliance with this Section 6.03
shall include having such provision specifically enforced by any court having equity jurisdiction,
it being acknowledged and agreed that any such breach or threatened breach shall cause irreparable
injury to Buyer and that money damages would not provide an adequate remedy to Buyer.
       (e)       Seller acknowledges that the actual damages likely to result from breach of this
Section 6.03 are difficult to estimate on the Effective Date and would be difficult for Buyer to
prove. Seller, therefore, agrees that in the event of a breach of this Section 6.03, Buyer shall be
entitled to recover five percent(5%)of the Purchase Price (the "Liquidated Damages Amount"),
plus all costs actually incurred,including all reasonable attorneys' fees, accounting fees, consulting
fees, and costs. The Parties agree that the Liquidated Damages Amount is reasonable, intend that
Seller's payment of the Liquidated Damages Amount would serve to compensate Buyer for any
breach by Seller of its obligations under this Section 6.03, and they do not intend for it to serve as
punishment for any such breach by Seller.

        Section 6.06 Confidentiality. From and after the Closing, each Party shall, and shall
cause its Affiliates to, hold, and shall use its reasonable best efforts to cause its or their respective
Representatives to hold, in confidence any and all information, whether written or oral, concerning
the Company, the other Party, or this Agreement, except to the extent that the disclosing Party can
show that such information (a) is available to the public through no breach of this Agreement by
the disclosing Party, any of its Affiliates or their respective Representatives; (b) is lawfully
acquired by Seller, any of its Affiliates, or their respective Representatives from and after the
Closing from sources which are not, to the disclosing Party's knowledge, prohibited from
disclosing such information by a legal, contractual, or fiduciary obligation; or(c) is independently
developed by the disclosing Party. If either Party or any of its Affiliates or their respective
Representatives reasonably believe that they are compelled to disclose any information by judicial
or administrative process or by other requirements of Law, the disclosing Party shall promptly
notify the non-disclosing Party in writing and shall disclose only that portion of such information
which the disclosing Party is advised by its counsel in writing is legally required to be disclosed;
provided, that the disclosing Party shall use reasonable best efforts to obtain an appropriate
protective order or other reasonable assurance that confidential treatment will be accorded such
information.

        Section 6.07 Non-competition; Non-solicitation.
       (a)      For a period of four (4) years commencing on the Closing Date (the "Non-
Competition    Restricted   Period"), Seller shall not, and shall not permit any of its Affiliates to,
directly or indirectly, (i) engage in or assist others in engaging in the Restricted Business in the


                                                   44

Territory; (ii) have an interest in any Person that engages directly or indirectly in the Restricted
Business in the Territory in any capacity, including as a partner, shareholder, member, employee,
principal, agent, trustee, or consultant; or (iii) intentionally interfere in any material respect with
the business relationships (whether formed prior to or after the Effective Date) between the
Company and customers or suppliers of the Company. Notwithstanding the foregoing, Seller and
Owner may own, directly or indirectly, solely as an investment, securities of any Person traded on
any national securities exchange if Seller is not a controlling Person of, or a member of a group
which controls, such Person and does not, directly or indirectly, own five percent(5%)or more of
any class of securities of such Person.
       (b)      For a period of two (2) years commencing on the Closing Date (the "Non-Solicit
Restricted   Period"),   Seller shall not, and shall not permit any of its Affiliates to, directly or
indirectly, hire or solicit any employee of the Company or encourage any such employee to leave
such employment or hire any such employee who has left such employment, except pursuant to a
general solicitation which is not directed specifically to any such employees; provided, that
nothing in this Section 6.07 shall prevent Seller or any of its Affiliates from hiring (i) after six
months from the date of termination of employment, any employee whose employment has been
terminated by the Company or Buyer or (ii) after one year from the date of termination of
employment, any employee whose employment has been terminated by the employee.

      (c)       During the Non-Competition Restricted Period, Seller shall not, and shall not
permit any of its Affiliates to, directly or indirectly, solicit or entice, or attempt to solicit or entice,
any clients or customers of the Company or any Person with whom the Company communicated
during the twelve (12) month period prior to the Closing Date for the purpose of soliciting that
Person as a new client or customer for purposes of diverting their business or services from the
Company.
       (d)      Seller acknowledges that a breach or threatened breach of this Section 6.07 would
give rise to irreparable harm to Buyer, for which monetary damages would not be an adequate
remedy, and hereby agrees that in the event of a breach or a threatened breach by Seller of any
such obligations, Buyer shall, in addition to any and all other rights and remedies that may be
available to it in respect of such breach, be entitled to equitable relief, including a temporary
restraining order, an injunction, specific performance, and any other relief that may be available
from a court of competent jurisdiction (without any requirement to post bond).

       (e)     Seller acknowledges that the restrictions contained in this Section 6.07 are
reasonable and necessary to protect the legitimate interests of Buyer and constitute a material
inducement to Buyer to enter into this Agreement and consummate the transactions contemplated
by this Agreement. In the event that any covenant contained in this Section 6.07 should ever be
adjudicated to exceed the time, geographic, product or service, or other limitations permitted by
applicable Law in any jurisdiction, then any court is expressly empowered to reform such
covenant, and such covenant shall be deemed reformed, in such jurisdiction to the maximum time,
geographic, product or service, or other limitations permitted by applicable Law. The covenants
contained in this Section 6.07 and each provision hereof are severable and distinct covenants and
provisions. The invalidity or unenforceability of any such covenant or provision as written shall

not invalidate or render unenforceable the remaining covenants or provisions hereof, and any such

                                          ARTICLE VII
                                          TAX MATTERS

        Section 7.01 Tax Covenants.
       (a)       Without the prior written consent of Buyer, Seller (and, prior to the Closing, the
Company, its Affiliates, and their respective Representatives) shall not, to the extent it may affect,
or relate to, the Company, make, change, or rescind any Tax election, amend any Tax Return, or
take any position on any Tax Return, take any action, omit to take any action, or enter into any
other transaction that is inconsistent with Seller's past practices and that would have the effect of
materially increasing the Tax liability or materially reducing any Tax asset of Buyer or the
Company in respect of any Post-Closing Tax Period. Subject to Section 7.01(b), Seller agrees that
Buyer is to have no liability for any Tax resulting from any action of Seller, or from any action of
the Company, its Affiliates, or any of their respective Representatives prior to the Closing, and
agrees to defend, indemnify, and hold harmless Buyer(and, after the Closing Date, the Company)
against any such Tax or reduction of any Tax asset.
      (b)      All transfer, documentary, sales, use, stamp, registration, value added, and other
such Taxes and fees (including any penalties and interest) incurred in connection with this
Agreement and the other Transaction Documents (including any real property transfer Tax and
any other similar Tax)shall be borne and paid by Buyer when due. Buyer shall, at its own expense,



                                                 48

===== PAGE 139 =====
timely file any Tax Return or other document with respect to such Taxes or fees (and Seller shall
cooperate with respect thereto as necessary).
         (c)     Buyer shall prepare, or cause to be prepared, all Tax Returns required to be filed by
 the Company after the Closing Date with respect to a Pre-Closing Tax Period other than the Seller
 Return. Any such Tax Return shall be prepared in a manner consistent with past practice (unless
 otherwise required by Law) and without a change of any election or any accounting method and
 shall be submitted by Buyer to Seller (together with schedules, statements and, to the extent
 requested by Seller, supporting documentation) at least forty-five (45) days prior to the due date
(including extensions) of such Tax Return. If Seller reasonably objects to any item on any such
 Tax Return, it shall, within ten(10)days after delivery of such Tax Return, notify Buyer in writing
 that it so objects, specifying with particularity any such item and stating the specific factual or
 legal basis for any such objection. If a notice of objection shall be duly delivered, Buyer and Seller
 shall negotiate in good faith and use their reasonable best efforts to resolve such items. If Buyer
 and Seller are unable to reach such agreement within ten (10) days after receipt by Buyer of such
 notice, the disputed items shall be resolved by the Independent Accountant and any determination
 by the Independent Accountant shall be final. The Independent Accountant shall resolve any
 disputed items within twenty(20)days of having the item referred to it pursuant to such procedures
 as it may require. If the Independent Accountant is unable to resolve any disputed items before the
 due date for such Tax Return, the Tax Return shall be filed as prepared by Buyer and then amended
 to reflect the Independent Accountant's resolution. The costs, fees, and expenses of the
 Independent Accountant shall be borne equally by Buyer and Seller. The preparation and filing of
 any Tax Return of the Company that does not relate to a Pre-Closing Tax Period shall be
 exclusively within the control of Buyer.
       (d)    Seller shall prepare, or cause to be prepared, all Tax Returns required to be filed by
 the Company prior to the Closing Date.
        (e)     Seller shall prepare, or cause to be prepared, all federal Tax Returns for Income
Taxes(and analogous state, local and foreign Tax Returns) and other Tax Returns of the Company
for all Tax periods ending on or prior to the Closing Date that are due after the Closing Date (each
such Tax Return prepared by Sellers, a "Seller Return"). Any such Seller Return shall be prepared
in a manner consistent with past practice (unless otherwise required by Law)and without a change
of any election or any accounting method and shall be submitted by Seller to Buyer (together with
schedules, statements and, to the extent requested by Buyer, supporting documentation) at least
forty-five (45) days prior to the due date (including extensions) of such Seller Return. If Buyer
reasonably objects to any item on any such Seller Return, it shall, within ten (10) days after
delivery of such Seller Return, notify Seller in writing that it so objects, specifying with
particularity any such item and stating the specific factual or legal basis for any such objection. If
a notice of objection shall be duly delivered, Buyer and Seller shall negotiate in good faith and use
their reasonable best efforts to resolve such items. If Buyer and Seller are unable to reach such
agreement within ten (10) days after receipt by Seller of such notice, the disputed items shall be
resolved by the Independent Accountant and any determination by the Independent Accountant
shall be final. The Independent Accountant shall resolve any disputed items within twenty (20)
days of having the item referred to it pursuant to such procedures as it may require. If the

                                                  49

===== PAGE 140 =====
Independent Accountant is unable to resolve any disputed items before the due date for such Seller
Return, the Seller Return shall be filed as prepared by Seller and then amended to reflect the
Independent Accountant's resolution. The costs, fees, and expenses of the Independent Accountant
shall be borne equally by Buyer and Seller.

        Section 7.02 Termination of Existing Tax Sharing Agreements. Any and all existing
Tax sharing agreements (whether written or not) binding upon the Company shall be terminated
as of the Closing Date. After such date neither the Company, Seller, nor any of Seller's Affiliates
and their respective Representatives shall have any further rights or liabilities thereunder.

         Section 7.03 Tax Indemnification. Seller shall defend and indemnify the Company,
 Buyer, and each Buyer Indemnitee and hold them harmless from and against (a) any Loss
 attributable to any breach of or inaccuracy in any representation or warranty made in Section 3.20;
(b) any Loss attributable to any breach or violation of, or failure to fully perform, any covenant,
 agreement, undertaking, or obligation in 0;(c) all Taxes of the Company or relating to the business
 of the Company for all Pre-Closing Tax Periods; (d) all Taxes of any member of an affiliated,
 consolidated, combined, or unitary group of which the Company (or any predecessor of the
 Company) is or was a member on or prior to the Closing Date by reason of a liability under
 Treasury Regulation Section 1.1502-6 or any comparable provisions offoreign, state, or local Law;
 and (e) any and all Taxes of any person imposed on the Company arising under the principles of
 transferee or successor liability or by Contract, relating to an event or transaction occurring before
 the Closing Date. Seller shall reimburse Buyer for any Taxes of the Company, that are the
 responsibility of Seller pursuant to clause (c) of this Section 7.03 within twenty (20) Business
 Days following payment of such Taxes by Buyer or the Company and the receipt by Seller of the
 applicable Tax Return filed by the Company together with proof of payment which shall include
 a copy of any check payable to the applicable Governmental Authority.

        Section 7.04 Straddle Period. In the case of Taxes that are payable with respect to a
 taxable period that begins before and ends after the Closing Date (each such period, a "Straddle
 Period"), the portion of any such Taxes that are treated as Pre-Closing Taxes for purposes of this
 Agreement shall be:
         (a)      in the case of Taxes (i) based upon, or related to, income, receipts, profits, wages,
 capital, or net worth;(ii) imposed in connection with the sale, transfer, or assignment of property;
 or (iii) required to be withheld, deemed equal to the amount which would be payable if the taxable
 year ended with the Closing Date; and
        (b)     in the case of other Taxes, deemed to be the amount of such Taxes for the entire
 period multiplied by a fraction the numerator of which is the number of days in the period ending
 on the Closing Date and the denominator of which is the number of days in the entire period.

         Section 7.05 Section 336(e) Election.
        (a)     Election. At the option of Buyer, the Company and Seller shall make a timely
 election under Section 336(e) of the Code (and any corresponding election under state, local, and

                                                  50

===== PAGE 141 =====
foreign Law) with respect to the purchase and sale of the Shares of the Company hereunder (a
"Section 336(e) Election") and shall execute a Section 336(e) election agreement as contemplated
by Treas. Reg. § 1.336-2(h)(3)(i) and in substantially the same form as attached hereto as Exhibit
C(the "Section 336(e)Election Agreement")and a Section 336(e) election statement as described
in Treas. Reg. § 1.336-2 and in substantially the same form as attached hereto as Exhibit D (the
"Section 336(e) Election Statement"). The obligations of Seller set forth in this Section 7.05(a)
shall survive the Closing. If a Section 336(e) Election is made, then the Seller shall include any
income, gain, loss, deduction or other tax item resulting from the Section 336(e) Election on their
Tax Returns to the extent required by applicable Law.
        (b)    Allocation of Purchase Price. If Buyer opts to cause the Company and Seller to
make the Section 336(e)Election, Seller and Buyer agree that the Purchase Price and the Liabilities
of the Company (plus other relevant items) shall be allocated among the assets of the Company
for all purposes (including Tax and financial accounting) as shown on an allocation schedule (the
"Allocation Schedule") substantially in the form attached hereto as Exhibit E. The Buyer, the
Seller and the Company agree to report consistently with the Allocation Schedule, except as
otherwise required by applicable Law.

        Section 7.06 Contests. Buyer agrees to give written notice to Seller of the receipt of any
written notice by the Company, Buyer, or any of Buyer's Affiliates which involves the assertion
ofany claim, or the commencement of any Action, in respect of which an indemnity may be sought
by Buyer pursuant to this 0(a "Tax Claim"); provided, that failure to comply with this provision
shall not affect Buyer's right to indemnification hereunder except and only to the extent that Seller
is prejudiced by reason of such failure. Buyer shall control the contest or resolution of any Tax
Claim; provided, however, that Buyer shall obtain the prior written consent of Seller (which
consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed) before entering into any settlement of a
claim or ceasing to defend such claim; and, provided further, that Seller shall be entitled to
participate in the defense of such claim and to employ counsel of its choice for such purpose, the
fees and expenses of which separate counsel shall be borne solely by Seller.

         Section 7.07 Cooperation and Exchange of Information. Seller and Buyer shall
 provide each other with such cooperation and information as either ofthem reasonably may request
 of the other in filing any Tax Return pursuant to this 0 or in connection with any audit or other
 proceeding in respect of Taxes of the Company. Such cooperation and information shall include
 providing copies of relevant Tax Returns or portions thereof, together with accompanying
 schedules, related work papers, and documents relating to rulings or other determinations by tax
 authorities. Each of Seller and Buyer shall retain all Tax Returns, schedules and work papers,
 records, and other documents in its possession relating to Tax matters of the Company for any
 taxable period beginning before the Closing Date until the expiration of the statute of limitations
 of the taxable periods to which such Tax Returns and other documents relate, without regard to
 extensions except to the extent notified by the other Party in writing of such extensions for the
 respective Tax periods. Prior to transferring, destroying, or discarding any Tax Returns, schedules
 and work papers, records, and other documents in its possession relating to Tax matters of the


                                                 51

===== PAGE 142 =====
Company for any taxable period beginning before the Closing Date, Seller or Buyer (as the case
may be) shall provide the other Party with reasonable written notice and offer the other Party the
opportunity to take custody of such materials.

       Section 7.08 Tax Treatment of Indemnification Payments. Any indemnification
payments pursuant to this 0 shall be treated as an adjustment to the Purchase Price by the Parties
for Tax purposes, unless otherwise required by Law.

       Section 7.09 Payments to Buyer. Any amounts payable to Buyer pursuant to this
Article VII shall be satisfied: (a) from the Indemnity Escrow Fund, and (b) to the extent such
amounts exceed the amount available to Buyer in the Indemnity Escrow Fund,from Seller.

        Section 7.10 Survival. Notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary, the
provisions of Section 3.20 and this 0 shall survive for the full period of all applicable statutes of
limitations (giving effect to any waiver, mitigation, or extension thereof) plus sixty (60) days.

        Section 7.11 Overlap. To the extent that any obligation or responsibility pursuant to
Article IX may overlap with an obligation or responsibility pursuant to this 0, the provisions of
this 0 shall govern.

                                          ARTICLE IX
                                        INDEMNIFICATION

        Section 9.01 Survival. Subject to the limitations and other provisions of this Agreement,
the representations and warranties contained herein (other than any representations or warranties
contained in Section 3.20, which are subject to 0) shall survive the Closing for two (2) years, or
until the date that is sixty(60) days following the expiration of the statute oflimitations applicable
to the underlying claim, whichever period is longer. All covenants and agreements of the Parties
contained herein (other than any covenants or agreements contained in 0, which are subject to 0)
shall survive the Closing indefinitely or for the period explicitly specified therein. Notwithstanding
the foregoing, any claims asserted in good faith with reasonable specificity (to the extent known
at such time) and in writing by notice from the non-breaching Party to the breaching Party prior to
the expiration date of the applicable survival period shall not thereafter be barred by the expiration
of the relevant representation or warranty and such claims shall survive until finally resolved.

        Section 9.02 Indemnification by Seller.
      (a)       Subject to the other terms and conditions of this Article IX, Seller shall indemnify
and defend each of Buyer and its Affiliates (including the Company) and their respective
Representatives (collectively, the "Buyer Indemnitees") against, and shall hold each of them
harmless from and against, and shall pay and reimburse each of them for, any and all Losses
incurred or sustained by, or imposed upon, Buyer Indemnitees based upon, arising out of, with
respect to or by reason of:
              (i)      any inaccuracy in or breach of any of the representations or warranties of
Seller contained in this Agreement or in any certificate or instrument delivered by or on behalf of
Seller pursuant to this Agreement (other than in respect of Section 3.20, it being understood that
the sole remedy for any such inaccuracy in or breach thereof shall be pursuant to 0), as of the date
such representation or warranty was made or as if such representation or warranty was made on
and as of the Closing Date (except for representations and warranties that expressly relate to a
specified date, the inaccuracy in or breach of which will be determined with reference to such
specified date);
                (ii)      any breach or non-fulfillment of any covenant, agreement, or obligation to
 be performed by Seller pursuant to this Agreement or in any of the other Transaction Documents
(other than any breach or violation of, or failure to fully perform, any covenant, agreement,
 undertaking, or obligation in 0, it being understood that the sole remedy for any such breach,
 violation, or failure shall be pursuant to 0);
               (iii)   Accrued Expenses;
             (iv)     any Liabilities or Losses related to any(A)Debt outstanding or arising prior
to the Closing, including Closing Debt, (B) Change of Control Payments, and (C) Company
Transaction Expenses; or
               (v)     any Closing Cash on Hand Shortfall;


                                                 55

===== PAGE 146 =====
               (vi)    any Debt of the Company related to the Excluded Assets; or
              (vii) all amounts of principal, interest, penalties or fees payable or that may
become payable under all loans that the Company has previously obtained under the Paycheck
Protection Program (the "PPP Loans"); any subsequent determination that forgiveness of any of
the PPP Loans was not appropriate (whether such determination applies to the entire amount of
any of the PPP Loans or some portion thereof); any failure by the Company to comply with the
CARES Act; and any Third Party Claims arising out of or relating to any of the foregoing.
"CARES Act" means the Coronavirus Aid, Relief, and Economic Security Act(Pub. L. 116-136),
the Families First Coronavirus Response Act of 2020 (H.R. 6201), "Division N - Additional
Coronavirus Response and Relief" of the Consolidated Appropriations Act, 2021 (H.R. 133) and
the American Rescue Plan Act of 2021 (Pub. L. 117-2), as applicable (including, in each case, any
changes in state or local Law that are analogous to provisions of the CARES Act or adopted to
conform to the CARES Act), and any legislative or regulatory guidance issued pursuant thereto.
      (b)       Notwithstanding anything contained herein to the contrary, all "material,"
"Material Adverse Effect" and similar materiality type qualifications contained in the
representations and warranties shall be ignored and not given any effect for the indemnification
provisions of this Agreement, including for purposes of(i) determining the amount of any Losses
incurred with respect to the indemnification provisions hereof, and (ii) determining whether or not
a breach of a representation or warranty has occurred.

        Section 9.03 Indemnification by Buyer. Subject to the other terms and conditions of
this Article IX, Buyer shall indemnify and defend each of Seller and its Affiliates and their
respective Representatives (collectively, the "Seller Indemnitees") against, and shall hold each of
them harmless from and against, and shall pay and reimburse each of them for, any and all Losses

       (a)     any inaccuracy in or breach of any of the representations or warranties of Buyer
contained in this Agreement or in any certificate or instrument delivered by or on behalf of Buyer
pursuant to this Agreement, as of the date such representation or warranty was made or as if such
representation or warranty was made on and as of the Closing Date (except for representations and
warranties that expressly relate to a specified date, the inaccuracy in or breach of which will be
determined with reference to such specified date);
        (b)      any breach or non-fulfillment of any covenant, agreement, or obligation to be
 performed by Buyer pursuant to this Agreement or in any of the other Transaction Documents
(other than 0, it being understood that the sole remedy for any such breach thereof shall be pursuant
 to 0); or

       (c)      any Closing Cash on Hand Surplus.

        Section 9.04 Indemnification Procedures. The Party making a claim under this Article
IX is referred to as the "Indemnified Party,'" and the Party against whom such claims are asserted
under this Article IX is referred to as the "Indemnifying Party."

                                                 56

===== PAGE 147 =====
         (a)    Third Party Claims. If any Indemnified Party receives notice of the assertion or
commencement of any Action made or brought by any Person who is not a party to this Agreement
or an Affiliate of a Party to this Agreement or a Representative of the foregoing (a "Third Party
 Claim") against such Indemnified Party with respect to which the Indemnifying Party is obligated
 to provide indemnification under this Agreement, the Indemnified Party shall give the
Indemnifying Party reasonably prompt written notice thereof, but in any event not later than thirty
(30) calendar days after receipt of such notice of such Third Party Claim. The failure to give such
 prompt written notice shall not, however, relieve the Indemnifying Party of its indemnification
 obligations, except and only to the extent that the Indemnifying Party is prejudiced by reason of
 such failure. Such notice by the Indemnified Party shall describe the Third Party Claim in
 reasonable detail, shall include copies of all material written evidence thereof, and shall indicate
 the estimated amount, if reasonably practicable, of the Loss that has been or may be sustained by
 the Indemnified Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have the right to participate in, or by giving
 written notice to the Indemnified Party, to assume the defense of any Third Party Claim at the
Indemnifying Party's expense and by the Indemnifying Party's own counsel, and the Indemnified
 Party shall cooperate in good faith in such defense; provided, that if the Indemnifying Party is
 Seller, such Indemnifying Party shall not have the right to defend or direct the defense of any such
 Third Party Claim that (i) is asserted directly by or on behalf of a Person that is a supplier or
 customer of the Company, or (ii) seeks an injunction or other equitable relief against the
 Indemnified Party. In the event that the Indemnifying Party assumes the defense of any Third Party
 Claim, subject to Section 9.04(b), it shall have the right to take such action as it deems necessary
 to avoid, dispute, defend, appeal, or make counterclaims pertaining to any such Third Party Claim
 in the name and on behalf of the Indemnified Party. The Indemnified Party shall have the right to
 participate in the defense of any Third Party Claim with counsel selected by it subject to the
 Indemnifying Party's right to control the defense thereof. The fees and disbursements of such
 counsel shall be at the expense of the Indemnified Party. If the Indemnifying Party elects not to
 compromise or defend such Third Party Claim, fails to promptly (and in any event no later than
 thirty (30) days following receipt of written notice of the Third Party Claim from the Indemnified
 Party) notify the Indemnified Party in writing of its election to defend as provided in this
 Agreement, or fails to diligently prosecute the defense of such Third Party Claim, the Indemnified
 Party may, subject to Section 9.04(b), pay, compromise, defend such Third Party Claim, and seek
 indemnification for any and all Losses based upon, arising from, or relating to such Third Party
 Claim, in each case subject to the terms and conditions of this Article IX. Seller and Buyer shall
 cooperate with each other in all reasonable respects in connection with the defense of any Third
 Party Claim,including making available(subject to the provisions ofSection 6.06)records relating
 to such Third Party Claim and furnishing, without expense (other than reimbursement of actual
  out-of-pocket expenses) to the defending Party, management employees of the non-defending
 Party as may be reasonably necessary for the preparation of the defense of such Third Party Claim.
       (b)     Settlement of Third Party Claims. Notwithstanding any other provision of this
 Agreement, the Indemnifying Party shall not enter into settlement of any Third Party Claim
 without the prior written consent of the Indemnified Party (which consent shall not be
 unreasonably withheld or delayed).


                                                 57

===== PAGE 148 =====
        (c)     Direct Claims. Any Action by an Indemnified Party on account of a Loss which
does not result from a Third Party Claim (a "Direct Claim") shall be asserted by the Indemnified
Party giving the Indemnifying Party reasonably prompt written notice thereof, but in any event not
later than thirty (30) days after the Indemnified Party becomes aware of such Direct Claim. The
failure to give such prompt written notice shall not, however, relieve the Indemnifying Party of its
indemnification obligations, except and only to the extent that the Indemnifying Party is prejudiced
by reason of such failure. Such notice by the Indemnified Party shall describe the Direct Claim in
reasonable detail, shall include copies of all material written evidence thereof and shall indicate
the estimated amount, if reasonably practicable, of the Loss that has been or may be sustained by
the Indemnified Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have thirty (30) days after its receipt of such
notice to respond in writing to such Direct Claim. The Indemnified Party shall allow the
Indemnifying Party and its professional advisors to investigate the matter or circumstance alleged
to give rise to the Direct Claim, and whether and to what extent any amount is payable in respect
of the Direct Claim and the Indemnified Party shall assist the Indemnifying Party's investigation
by giving such information and assistance (including access to the Company's premises and
personnel and the right to examine and copy any accounts, documents, or records) as the
Indemnifying Party or any ofits professional advisors may reasonably request. Ifthe Indemnifying
Party does not so respond within such thirty (30) day period, the Indemnifying Party shall be
deemed to have rejected such claim, in which case the Indemnified Party shall be free to pursue
such remedies as may be available to the Indemnified Party on the terms and subject to the
provisions of this Agreement.
       (d)       Tax Claims. Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, the control
of any claim,  assertion, event, or proceeding in respect of Taxes of the Company (including, but
not limited to, any such claim in respect of a breach of the representations and warranties in Section
3.20 hereof or any breach or violation of or failure to fully perform any covenant, agreement,
undertaking, or obligation in 0) shall be governed exclusively by 0 hereof.
       (e)     Accounts Receivable. If Buyer provides notice of any Third Party Claim or Direct
Claim, Buyer shall be allowed, in its discretion, to retain all amounts collected with respect to
Accounts Receivable. All such collections made after the date of the notice shall be the property
of Buyer but shall be held by Buyer until resolution of such Third Party Claim or Direct Claim, at
which time ownership of any such amounts collected to which Seller is otherwise entitled under
Section 2.08 in excess of such Third Party Claim or Direct Claim shall become the property of
Seller. Further, without providing notice to Seller, Buyer may use up to $100,000 from the
Indemnity Escrow Fund as cash on hand to cover any unpaid Accrued Expenses for the period of
three (3) months following the Closing Date. Should any Accrued Expenses remain after three (3)
months following the Closing Date, such expenses shall become a claim against the Indemnity
Escrow Fund.

        Section 9.05 Payments.

       (a)    Once a Loss is agreed to by the Indemnifying Party or finally adjudicated to be
payable pursuant to this Article IX, the Indemnifying Party shall satisfy its obligations within
fifteen (15) Business Days of such final, non-appealable adjudication by wire transfer of

                                                 58

===== PAGE 149 =====
immediately available funds. The Parties agree that should an Indemnifying Party not make full
payment of any such obligations within such fifteen(15)Business Day period, any amount payable
shall accrue interest from and including the date of agreement of the Indemnifying Party or final,
non-appealable adjudication to and including the date such payment has been made at a rate per
annum equal to eight percent (8%). Such interest shall be calculated daily on the basis of a 365-
day year and the actual number of days elapsed, without compounding.
      (b)      Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, any amount to which Buyer becomes entitled
by reason of the provisions of this Article IX,0, or Section 2.05 shall be paid or satisfied by Seller
as provided in the following subsections:
                       First, for so long as there are funds in the Indemnity Escrow Fund
maintained under the Escrow Agreement, any and all amounts payable by Seller as an
Indemnifying Party to Buyer Indemnitees will be paid in cash out of the Indemnity Escrow Fund
established pursuant to this Agreement and the Escrow Agreement.
              (ii)     Second, to the extent such claim has not otherwise been satisfied pursuant
to Section 9.05(b)(i), Seller shall pay such Losses by certified or bank check in immediately
available funds or by wire transfer to an account designated by Buyer.

      Section 9.06 Tax Treatment of Indemnification Payments. All indemnification
payments made under this Agreement shall be treated by the Parties as an adjustment to the
Purchase Price for Tax purposes, unless otherwise required by Law.

        Section 9.07 Effect of Investigation. The representations, warranties, and covenants of
the Indemnifying Party, and the Indemnified Party's right to indemnification with respect thereto,
shall not be affected or deemed waived by reason of any investigation made by or on behalf of the
Indemnified Party (including by any of its Representatives) or by reason of the fact that the
Indemnified Party or any of its Representatives had actual knowledge that any such representation
or warranty is or was inaccurate or by reason of the Indemnified Party's waiver of any condition
set forth in Section 8.02 or Section 8.03, as the case may be.

        Section 9.08 Other Rights and Remedies Not Affected. The indemnification rights of
the Parties under this Article IX are independent of, and in addition to, such rights and remedies
as the Parties may have at Law or in equity or otherwise for any misrepresentation, breach of
warranty or failure to fulfill any covenant, agreement, or obligation hereunder on the part of any
Party, including the right to seek specific performance, rescission or restitution, none of which
rights or remedies shall be affected or diminished hereby.

       Section 9.09 Indemnification Limitations.        The indemnification obligations
contemplated by this Article IX will be subject to the following additional limitations and
conditions.
     (a)     Seller shall not be liable for payment of any indemnification obligation under this
Agreement unless and until the Indemnity Escrow Fund has been exhausted; and


                                                 59

===== PAGE 150 =====
       (b)     in no event will an Indemnified Party be entitled to recover or make a claim for (i)
consequential, special or indirect damages that are remote or speculative damages and not the
natural, probable and reasonably foreseeable consequence of the event that gave rise thereto or the
matter for which indemnification is sought hereunder,(ii) lost profits, or (iii) punitive damages.

      Section 9.10 Effectiveness. The provisions of this Article IX shall be effective upon
consummation of the Closing, and prior to the Closing shall have no force and effect.

                                            ARTICLE X
                                            TERMINATION

       Section 10.01 Termination. This Agreement may be terminated at any time prior to the
Closing:
       (a)      by the mutual written consent of Seller and Buyer;
       (b)      by Buyer by written notice to Seller if:
               (i)      Buyer is not fully satisfied, in its sole discretion, with the results of its due
diligence investigation regarding the Company; provided, however, that the termination right in
this Section 10.01(b)(i) shall be deemed waived if Buyer has not provided written notice to Seller
that Buyer is not satisfied with its diligence investigation by 11:59pm on September 30, 2021.
              (ii)     Buyer is not then in breach of any provision of this Agreement and there
has been a breach, inaccuracy in, or failure to perform any representation, warranty, covenant, or
agreement made by Seller pursuant to this Agreement that would give rise to the failure of any of
the conditions specified in Article VIII and such breach, inaccuracy, or failure has not been cured
by Seller within ten (10) days of Seller's receipt of written notice of such breach from Buyer; or
       (c)      by Seller by written notice to Buyer if:
                        Seller is not then in breach of any provision of this Agreement and there
has been a material breach of any representation or warranty made by Buyer pursuant to this
Agreement that would give rise to the failure of any of the conditions specified in Article VIII
and such material breach has not been cured by Buyer within thirty (30) days of Buyer's receipt
of written notice of such breach from Seller; or
       (d)     by Buyer or Seller in the event that (i) there shall be any Law that makes
consummation of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement illegal, or (ii) any
Governmental Authority shall have issued a Governmental Order restraining or enjoining the
transactions contemplated by this Agreement, and such Governmental Order shall have become
final and non-appealable.

        Section 10.02 Effect of Termination. In the event of the termination of this Agreement
in accordance with this Article, this Agreement shall forthwith become void and there shall be no
liability on the part of any Party except:
       (a)      as set forth in this Article X and Section 6.06 and Article XI hereof; and

                                                  60

===== PAGE 151 =====
       (b)     that nothing herein shall relieve any Party from liability for any willful breach of
any provision hereof.
