        Section 11.03 Interpretation. For purposes of this Agreement,(a) the words "include,"
"includes," and "including" shall be deemed to be followed by the words "without limitation";(b)
the word "or" is not exclusive; and (c) the words "herein," "hereof," "hereby," "hereto," and
"hereunder" refer to this Agreement as a whole. Unless the context otherwise requires, references
herein:(i) to Articles, Sections, Disclosure Schedules, and Exhibits mean the Articles and Sections
of, and Disclosure Schedules, and Exhibits attached to, this Agreement; (ii) to an agreement,
instrument, or other document means such agreement, instrument, or other document as amended,
supplemented, and modified from time to time to the extent permitted by the provisions thereof
and (iii) to a statute means such statute as amended from time to time and includes any successor
legislation thereto and any regulations promulgated thereunder. This Agreement shall be construed
without regard to any presumption or rule requiring construction or interpretation against the Party
drafting an instrument or causing any instrument to be drafted. The Disclosure Schedules and
Exhibits referred to herein shall be construed with, and as an integral part of, this Agreement to
the same extent as if they were set forth verbatim herein.

        Section 11.04 Headings. The headings in this Agreement are for reference only and shall
not affect the interpretation of this Agreement.

        Section 11.05 Severability. If any term or provision of this Agreement is invalid, illegal,
or unenforceable in any jurisdiction, such invalidity, illegality, or unenforceability shall not affect
any other term or provision of this Agreement or invalidate or render unenforceable such term or
provision in any other jurisdiction. Additionally, if any of the provisions of this Agreement are
held unenforceable by a court ofcompetent jurisdiction, then the Parties desire that such provision,
clause, or phrase be "blue-penciled" or rewritten by the court to the extent necessary to render it
enforceable.

        Section 11.06 Entire Agreement. This Agreement and the other Transaction Documents
constitute the sole and entire agreement of the Parties with respect to the subject matter contained
herein and therein, and supersede all prior and contemporaneous understandings, and agreements,


                                                 62

===== PAGE 153 =====
both written and oral, with respect to such subject matter. In the event of any inconsistency between
the statements in the body of this Agreement and those in the other Transaction Documents, the
Exhibits, and Disclosure Schedules (other than an exception expressly set forth as such in the
Disclosure Schedules), the statements in the body of this Agreement will control.

        Section 11.07 Successors and Assigns. This Agreement shall be binding upon and shall
inure to the benefit of the Parties and their respective successors and permitted assigns. Neither
Party may assign its rights or obligations hereunder without the prior written consent of the other
Party, which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed; provided, however, that prior
to the Closing Date, Buyer may, without the prior written consent of Seller, assign all or any
portion of its rights under this Agreement to one(1)or more of its direct or indirect wholly owned
subsidiaries or to any of its Affiliates. No assignment shall relieve the assigning Party of any of its
obligations hereunder.

        Section 11.08 No Third-Party Beneficiaries. Except as provided in Section 7.03 and
Article IX, this Agreement is for the sole benefit of the Parties and their respective successors and
permitted assigns and nothing herein, express or implied, is intended to or shall confer upon any
other Person any legal or equitable right, benefit, or remedy of any nature whatsoever under or by
reason of this Agreement.

        Section 11.09 Amendment and Modification; Waiver. This Agreement may only be
amended, modified, or supplemented by an agreement in writing signed by each Party. No waiver
by any Party of any of the provisions hereof shall be effective unless explicitly set forth in writing
and signed by the Party so waiving. No waiver by any Party shall operate or be construed as a
waiver in respect of any failure, breach, or default not expressly identified by such written waiver,
whether of a similar or different character, and whether occurring before or after that waiver. No
failure to exercise, or delay in exercising, any right, remedy, power, or privilege arising from this
Agreement shall operate or be construed as a waiver thereof; nor shall any single or partial exercise
of any right, remedy, power, or privilege hereunder preclude any other or further exercise thereof
or the exercise of any other right, remedy, power, or privilege.

        Section 11.10 Governing Law; Submission to Jurisdiction; Waiver of Jury Trial.

       (a)      This Agreement shall be interpreted and construed in accordance with the Laws of
the State of North Carolina. Any and all claims, controversies, and causes of action arising out of
or relating to this Agreement, whether sounding in contract, tort, or statute, shall be governed by
the Laws of the State of North Carolina, including its statutes of limitations, without giving effect
to any conflict-of-laws rule that would result in the application of the Laws of a different
jurisdiction.
    (b)   ANY LEGAL SUIT, ACTION, OR PROCEEDING ARISING OUT OF OR
BASED UPON THIS AGREEMENT,THE OTHER TRANSACTION DOCUMENTS,OR THE
TRANSACTIONS CONTEMPLATED HEREBY OR THEREBY MAY BE INSTITUTED IN
THE FEDERAL COURTS OF THE UNITED STATES OF AMERICA OR THE COURTS

                                                  63

===== PAGE 154 =====
LOCATED IN WAKE COUNTY,NORTH CAROLINA,AND EACH PARTY IRREVOCABLY
SUBMITS TO THE EXCLUSIVE JURISDICTION OF SUCH COURTS IN ANY SUCH SUIT,
ACTION, OR PROCEEDING. SERVICE OF PROCESS, SUMMONS, NOTICE, OR OTHER
DOCUMENT BY MAIL TO SUCH PARTY'S ADDRESS SET FORTH HEREIN SHALL BE
EFFECTIVE SERVICE OF PROCESS FOR ANY SUIT, ACTION,OR OTHER PROCEEDING
BROUGHT IN ANY SUCH COURT. THE PARTIES IRREVOCABLY AND
UNCONDITIONALLY WAIVE ANY OBJECTION TO THE LAYING OF VENUE OF ANY
SUIT, ACTION, OR ANY PROCEEDING IN SUCH COURTS AND IRREVOCABLY WAIVE
AND AGREE NOT TO PLEAD OR CLAIM IN ANY SUCH COURT THAT ANY SUCH SUIT,
ACTION, OR PROCEEDING BROUGHT IN ANY SUCH COURT HAS BEEN BROUGHT IN
AN INCONVENIENT FORUM.

        Section 11.11 Specific Performance. The Parties agree that irreparable damage would
occur if any provision of this Agreement were not performed in accordance with the terms hereof
and, subject to any limitations otherwise provided in this Agreement, including without limitation
the limitations provided in Section 9.09, that Buyer shall be entitled to specific performance of the
terms hereof, in addition to any other remedy to which it is entitled at law or in equity.

       Section 11.12 Survival. All covenants, agreements, and provisions herein that by their
terms extend past the Closing Date shall survive the execution and delivery of this Agreement and
any transactions contemplated hereby until fully performed in accordance with their terms.

        Section 11.13 Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in counterparts, each of
which shall be deemed an original, but all of which together shall be deemed to be one and the
same agreement. A signed copy of this Agreement delivered by facsimile, e-mail, or other means
of electronic transmission shall be deemed to have the same legal effect as delivery of an original
signed copy of this Agreement.

                                                  Lease Terms

Landlord:                 ADL Properties, LLC

Property Address:         1049 Park Ave, Cranston, RI 02910

Leased space:             6,000 square feet comprising entire second floor of property

General Description of leased space:

Home Health office occupies entire top floor of 2-story building. Approximately 6000 sq ft, includes
reception area, 2 conference rooms, 2 bathrooms, kitchen/lunchroom, and 7 partitioned office spaces
(not fully enclosed). Please see pictures in data room folder: Facility/Pictures.

There is an adult day care on the first floor. The second floor is accessed via stairs. There is no elevator.
The first floor has handicap access to bathrooms and conference rooms near the main entrance of the
building. These may be utilized as needed by the home health agency.

Space Classification:     Class B

Zoning:                   Building is approved for business office use as well as adult care use



Rate/Terms:

    •     $5,850 per month (equates to $11.70 a sq ft a year)
    •     Rent will include taxes, gas, water/sewer, electric, trash, snow removal and common area
          maintenance and cleaning.
    •     Tenant will carry tenant insurance on leased space.
    •     Owner will carry owner's policy on building.
    •     Initial 2-to-3-year term, with option to renew with a graduated rent increase of 2% per year.

                          SECTION 336(e) ELECTION AGREEMENT

       This SECTION 336(e) ELECTION AGREEMENT,dated as of[•](this "Agreement"), is
entered into by and among Hope Nursing Home Care, LLC a Rhode Island limited liability
company(the "Company")and Irene Aiyun Qi, an individual resident of Massachusetts ("Seller").

        Reference is hereby made to that certain Membership Interest Purchase Agreement (the
"Purchase Agreement") entered into by and among Community Based Care, LLC, a Delaware
limited liability company ("Buyer"), the Company and Seller on [•]. Pursuant to the Purchase
Agreement, Buyer is purchasing all of the outstanding equity interests in the Company, which
purchase constitutes a "qualified stock disposition" within the meaning of Section 1.336-1(b)(6)
of the Treasury Regulations.

         In accordance with Section 1.336-2(h)(3)(i) of the Treasury Regulations, each of the
 undersigned hereby agree to make a Section 336(e) election (as defined in Section 1.336-1(b)(11)
 of the Treasury Regulations) with respect to the purchase and sale of the equity interests in the
 Company pursuant to the Purchase Agreement. Additionally, the Company agrees to attach the
 Section 336(e) election statement described under Section 1.336-2(h)(5) and (6) to its timely filed
(including extensions) U.S. federal income tax return, as well as applicable state returns, for the
 taxable year that includes the qualified stock disposition.

                                                                             Execution Version


                                      Schedule 3.04

                                 No Conflicts; Consents

1. Home Nursing Care Provider License/Change in Effective Control Review — Rhode Island
   law, regulations and sub-regulatory guidance require the approval of an application for
   change of effective control ("CEC") in advance of a change in owner of a home nursing
   care provider, which is defined by state law to include "any change in membership that
   results in a new person acquiring a controlling vote in the [non]business corporation." See
   R.I. Stat. § 23-17-2 (4)(iii)(E); 216-RICR-40-10-17.4.2.B.

    The CEC review process requires the filing of an application with the Rhode Island
    Department of Health ("RIDOH"). Once reviewed and deemed complete by RIDOH staff,
    the CEC application is subject to formal review by the Health Services Council
   ("Council"). If approved by the Council, the Council report is subject to final approval by
    the Director of RIDOH who may approve the CEC, approve the CEC with conditions or
    deny the CEC application. If approved, the Applicant submits updated ownership
    information to the Facilities Regulation licensing division and a new license is issued,
    typically to coincide with date of closing of the transaction.

2. Rhode Island Medicaid — Guidance indicates that a Provider Change of Information Form
   must be filed with Gainwell Technologies, the administrative contractor that handles
   provider enrollment for Rhode Island Medicaid within 35 days of a change in ownership.
  (form       accessed       here:       htlps://cohlis.ri.vov/sitesiefiles/x1(Our226/files/2021-
   031provcoi 04201). The Rhode Island Medicaid Provider Enrollment User Guide defines a
   change in ownership to include "any change in membership, which results in a new person
   acquiring a controlling vote in the [nonbusiness] corporation." (accessed here:
   https://ww w.rjzovidedr )orta I.ori2LHCP/hp/uslic/docs/provider/Prov iclerEnrollmentUserCiu
   ide en-us.pdf)

                                     Schedule 4.02

                                No Conflicts; Consents


1. Home Nursing Care Provider License/Change in Effective Control Review — Rhode Island
   law, regulations and sub-regulatory guidance require the approval of an application for
   change in effective control ("CEC") in advance of a change in owner of a home nursing
   care provider, which is defined by state law to include "any change in membership that
   results in a new person acquiring a controlling vote in the [non]business corporation." See
   R.I. Stat. § 23-17-2 (4)(iii)(E); 216-RICR-40-10-17.4.2.B.

   The CEC review process requires the filing of an application with the Rhode Island
   Department of Health ("RIDOH"). Once reviewed and deemed complete by RIDOH staff,
   the CEC application is subject to formal review by the Health Services Council
  ("Council"). If approved by the Council, the Council report is subject to final approval by
   the Director of RIDOH who may approve the CEC, approve the CEC with conditions or
   deny the CEC application. If approved, the Applicant submits updated ownership
   information to the Facilities Regulation licensing division and a new license is issued,
   typically to coincide with date of closing of the transaction.


2. Rhode Island Medicaid — Guidance indicates that a Provider Change of Information Form
   must be filed with Gainwell Technologies, the administrative contractor that handles
   provider enrollment for Rhode Island Medicaid within 35 days of a change in ownership.
  (form       accessed      here:    Iltu2s://eohhs.ri.o.:ov/sites/a/files/Alw226/files/2021
   01prqycpijag). The Rhode Island Medicaid Provider Enrollment User Guide defines a
   change in ownership to include "any change in membership, which results in a new person
   acquiring a controlling vote in the [nonbusiness] corporation." (accessed here:
   htt6s://www.riproviderportal.org/HCP/Im/ushc/docs/provider/ProviderEnrollmentUserGu
   ide en-us.pdf)

===== PAGE 225 =====
                                      CBC Charity Care Policy

        PURPOSE
        To identify the criteria to be applied when accepting patients for charity care

        POLICY
        Patients without third-party payer coverage and who are unable to pay for medically
        necessary care will be accepted for charity care admission, per established criteria.
        Community Based Care will establish objective criteria and financial screening
        procedures for determining eligibility for charity care.
        The organization will consistently apply the Financial Assistance policy.
         PROCEDURE
        When it is identified that the patient has no source for payment of services and requires
        medically necessary care/service, the patient must provide personal financial
        information upon which the determination of charity care will be made.

        An administrator will meet with the patient to determine potential eligibility for financial
        assistance from other community resources.

        The administrator, along with the appropriate Program Director, will review all
        applicable patient information, including financial declarations, physician (or other
        authorized licensed independent practitioner) orders, initial assessment information,
        and notes to determine acceptance for charity care.

        An initial application will be completed by the patient and/or their legal representative
        with information which will be used to determine the patient's acceptance for charity
        care.

        The application will be submitted to the Chief Financial Officer/Business office
        designee for approval.

        All documentation utilized in the determination for acceptance for charity care will be
        maintained in the patient's billing record.

        When financial declarations reveal the patient is able to make partial payment for
        services, the Administrator, with the appropriate program director, will determine the
        sliding-fee schedule to be implemented.

        The revised sliding-fee schedule will be presented to the patient for agreement and
        signature.




61312309 v2

===== PAGE 226 =====
        After acceptance for charity care, the patient's ability to pay will be reassessed every
        60-90 days.

        When the organization is unable to admit the patient or to continue charity care, every
        effort will be made to refer the patient for appropriate care/service with an alternate
        provider.

        The referral source will be advised of acceptance, non-acceptance, continuation, or
        discharge from charity care.

        A patient/legal representative wishing to appeal the financial assistance determination
        can submit an appeal in writing to the Chief Financial Officer by email at:
        Imcdanie10..cbcare.com or by fax to (919) 256-0833. The CFO will review
        information and provide final determination within five (5) business days of receipt of
        the appeal letter.




61312309 v2

===== PAGE 228 =====
                               HOPE NURSING HOME CARE




                                    Quality Assurance Plan

                                    CONFIDENTIALITY

Documentation and communication used in Quality Assurance activities is confidential. All data
collection, measurement, and reporting improvement activities will not contain any identifiable
patient or staff information, in accordance with HIPAA regulation. All reports from Quality
Assurance activities remain the property ofthe agency. Information whether patient or proprietary,
is released only with proper authorization.

===== PAGE 229 =====
I. Quality Assurance Plan Objectives

The Quality Assurance Plan is designed to ensure high quality care and customer satisfaction in
all areas of practice, care, and service delivery. Performance is continuously measured to increase
patient safety, prevent and reduce adverse events and the incidence of sentinel events.

The Quality Assurance process shall be a team based process that systematically measures,
assesses and improves the organization's performance to improve patient and organizational
outcomes. The performance measurement process will provide a structure that will ensure all
employees are aligned and focused on the performance needs ofthe organization and on improving
performance capabilities.

    1. Reflect the Mission in all organizational activities.

   2. Improve efficiency and effectiveness of patient care and services by collecting data to
      monitor its performance.

   3. Meet and exceed patient and referral source expectations.

   4. Continually improve staff and patient knowledge and understanding regarding the use of
      equipment, supplies, and care.

   5. Improve and enhance the standards of service and care utilizing a systematic performance
      improvement approach.

   6. Identify areas for improvement in the operational, clinical, and financial policies and
      procedures and implement a plan for improvement that incorporates principles of
      continuous quality improvement(CQI).
   7. An ongoing, proactive program for identifying and reducing unanticipated adverse events
      and safety risks to patients is defined and implemented.

II. Leadership

Leadership has active involvement and participation in the PI plan. The leadership is
knowledgeable in Quality Assurance processing and methodologies.

This is evidenced by education, reading, and involvement in the development of the written plan
for Quality Assurance and activities, attendance and active participation at the Performance
Improvement meetings.

The leaders set performance improvement activities and identify how the organization adjusts
priorities in response to unusual or urgent events.

Patient services are provided through a collaborative effort of all members of the agency. The
leadership ensures that all staff are dedicated and trained in the processing and methodologies of

                                                 2

===== PAGE 230 =====
performance improvement.

Priorities for monitoring activities will be established through the Quality Assurance meetings.
Prioritizing of Quality Assurance activities will be based on high risk, high volume and problem
prone activities. Activities will focus on patient outcomes, but will also include structure and
process outcomes.

Resources for an organizational wide Quality Assurance program are allocated through the
assignment of staff and the provision oftime for staffto participate in Quality Assurance activities.
Additional resources are provided through development and use of data collection tools, clearly
defined reporting mechanisms, education in the development of indicators, and analysis of results.
To enhance the program Leadership will actively participate at the Quality Assurance meetings
and foster communication of activities and results throughout the agency. Leadership is ultimately
responsible for overseeing and directing the organization's Quality Assurance program.

III. Scope of Care and Services

Hope Nursing Home Care provides personal care and homemaking and community resource
coordination to a population of patients that includes but is not limited to the adult, pediatric and
disabled population.

Services are provided to patients of all ages, regardless of race, color and creed, sex, sexual
orientation, national or ethnic origin or payment source.

The care, services and equipment provided may include but are not limited to:
       Personal Care
       Homemaking

IV. Major Functions and Processes

Patient Focused Functions
• Rights & Responsibilities of the Individual
• Provision of Care and Services
• Surveillance, Prevention and Control of Infection
• Record of Care and Services

Organizational Functions:
• Improving Organization Performance
• Leadership
• Environmental Safety
• Emergency Management
• Management of Human Resources
• Information Management
• National Patient Safety Goals

Major functions and processes are monitored to reflect the agency's provision of care and
service. They are reflective of those that are most important to the health and safety of patients

                                                  3

===== PAGE 231 =====
served. Each major functional process will be measured to determine the existing level of
performance so that informed judgments can be made about the stability of the existing
processes, opportunities for improvement can be identified, the process can be redesigned, and
the redesigned process can be measured to see it meets the organization's objectives.

A high priority will be placed on monitoring those functions and processes which:
1. Occur frequently or affect large numbers of patients.
2. May place patients at risk for serious consequences or deprivation of substantial benefit
   when:
   a. care/service is not provided correctly
   b. care/service is not provided when indicated
   c. care/service is provided when not indicated
   d. produce problems for patients or staff

Also reviewed will be: healthcare trends that warrant study, particularly in relation to quality of care
issues, actual/potential adverse healthcare outcomes that warrant study, improvement in delivery of
healthcare that would have a positive impact on patient care and/or clinical practice and benefits for
client care and/or clinical practice that are worthy of expenditures to implement study.

 V. Performance Measures

 Measurement is the foundation for performance improvement. Indicators are objective,
 measurable, based on current knowledge and clinical experience and identify opportunities for
 improvement. Performance measures reflect structures of care (resources), process of care
(procedures, techniques) or outcomes of care (complications, or patient progress). Performance
 measures focus on all major functions and processes of the agency.

Priorities are set to determine the processes and activities that have the greatest significance in
fulfilling the Mission, the needs/expectations of patients, staff, and the community. Performance
activities are re-prioritized as needed in response to unusual or urgent events.

Performance benchmarking is an on-going activity designed to encourage continuous
improvement in all processes by setting target goals according to identified "best practices."

Performance measures for processes that are known to jeopardize the safety of patients or are
associated with sentinel events will be routinely monitored. At a minimum, performance
measures related to the following processes are monitored:

    • Management of hazardous conditions
    • National patient satisfaction and safety goals


Benchmarks or thresholds that trigger intensive assessment and evaluation are established.

VI. Systematic Performance Improvement Approach

A. Measurement

                                                 4

===== PAGE 232 =====
Data collection is continuously performed to monitor the stability of existing processes, identify
opportunities for improvement, identify changes that lead to improvement or sustain
improvement. Data collection is based on the frequency of the activity monitored; the
significance of the activity and the extent to which the activity is monitored is problem free.
Sources of data collection include, but are not limited to:

   • Staff needs and suggestions for improving patient safety
   • Staff willingness to reports unanticipated adverse events
   • Environmental conditions
   • Patient needs, expectations, and satisfaction
   • Perception of Care Surveys
   • Patient letters and/or comments regarding services
   • Risk management
   • Quality control
   • Infection control
   • Utilization Review
   • Record Review
   • FMEA
   • Accident/incident reports
   • Performance improvement activity form
   • Structure, process and outcome study data
   • Staff orientation, in-service and continuing education records
   • Inter/Intradepartmental committee meeting minutes
   • Formal group meetings
   • Informal group meetings
   • Accreditation/licensure/certification survey requirements
   • Client classification data
   • Client care standards
   • Personnel records
   • Financial reports
   • Other data relating directly/indirectly to client care
   • External Data sources, including but not limited to the following:
             Professional organizations
             Regulatory agencies
             Continuing education conferences
         • Professional conferences
         • Professional literature




Indicators are developed based on current knowledge and best practice. Reliable performance
thresholds are established for each indicator, against which current and future performance is
evaluated. Thresholds are based on current knowledge, staff experience, past performances,

                                                5

===== PAGE 233 =====
patient needs and expectations, external benchmarks, and the degree of seriousness of the
identified improvement process.

Data collection is used to determine actions needed to improve processes and whether or not they
were effective. Examples of various actions are: redesigning of a process, implementing a new
process, changes in policies and procedures, changes in communication chains, changes in
organizational structures, changes in documentation and forms, and changes in in-service and
training practices. Where undesirable variation in performance occurs, intensive assessments are
initiated. These may include:

• Sentinel events — Sentinel events are defined as an unexpected occurrence or variation
  involving death or serious physical or psychological injury, or the risk thereof. Serious
  injury specifically includes loss of limb or function. The phrase "or risk thereof" includes
  any process variation for which a re-occurrence would carry a significant chance of a serious
  adverse outcome.
• variance of outcomes from desired levels
• hazardous conditions


The agency strives to proactively identify and reduce risks to the patients and staff it serves; with
the goal of preventing adverse events. In order to ensure patient safety, at least one high-risk
process will be selected annually for analysis. A high-risk process is defined as one that if not
planned or implemented correctly, has significant potential for impacting the safety of the
patient. The analysis will be performed using a "failure mode" methodology and should include:
    • Description of the process, through flow charting
    • Identification of the ways in which the process could breakdown or fail,
    • Identification of the possible effects the failure of the process could have on patient,
    • Prioritizing the possible failures,.
    • Determining why the prioritized failures could occur,
    • Redesigning the process or underlying systems to minimize the risk to patients,
    • Implementation and ongoing monitoring of the redesigned process.




The PDCA approach is utilized as a performance improvement approach to assist the
organizational Performance Improvement activities.

                                                 6

===== PAGE 234 =====
PDCA includes four phases of problem solving to achieve Performance Improvement:

Plan:          Define the problem
               Determine the impact if problem not resolved
               Define desired outcome

Do:            Decide what you need to know
               Collect data
               Assess and Interpret data
               Define most influential factors
               Identify root causes of problem

Check/Study: Develop a solution
             Develop an implementation plan
Act:         Gain commitment from management
             Execute the plan
             Measure and record performance to determine impact


B. Assessment

Data is aggregated and analyzed to draw conclusions about the organization's performance and
to determine what is done, how well services and care are provided and if performance is
acceptable. Aggregation and analysis is performed at a frequency appropriate to the activity or
process being evaluated, using statistical tools and techniques. The results of the data analysis
are compared internally over time and with external sources when available.

The following are questions that, as applicable, should be asked during the assessment phase.

a. What is the current level of performance?
b. Is the level of performance within acceptable standards or predefined performance
   expectations as established by policy and procedure and operational practices, and other
   similar organizations or databases?
c. Is the level of performance consistent with the company mission?
d. Are there patterns or trends developing that indicate improvement in a process may be
   required?
e. Do the performance measures indicate that changes or previously implemented solutions
   resulted in an improvement in performance?
f. Do the Satisfaction Survey responses indicate that patients perceive that their needs were
   met?
                                                7

===== PAGE 235 =====
g. Is the level of performance the same as, better than or less than the previous month or
   quarter?
h. Is performance acceptable, but there is a desire and ability to continue to make
   improvements?
i. Is there a single event that is significant or important enough to require more intensive
   investigation and/or result in a redesign of a current process or practice?
k. Are there confirmed equipment defects or failures that resulted in injury or death to the
   patient and have been identified as sentinel event?
1. What do outcome performance measures indicate about current care processes, what we do
   and how we provide care and services, in relation to the dimensions of performance?

Indicators are continually assessed with respect to customer service, the current professional
practice standards, and continued relevance. To foster improvement opportunities the
organization encourages the use of internal and external benchmarking.

C. Improvement

The systematic performance improvement approach, PDSA is used to identify an opportunity for
improvement or problems. Once an opportunity or problem is identified, an Action Plan is
developed and implemented. Action Plans should determine what is needed to accomplish the
desired outcome, identify the person(s) responsible, tasks to be accomplished, and the target date
for completion. Input should be obtained from all individuals involved in the process to assure
effective solutions are implemented.

An Action Plan is:
  • written in clear, concrete language; describes the process or outcome the is to be
      improved;
  • relates to best practices;
  • practical and achievable; and
  • specific to the identified problem.

If a significant problem or new improvement priority is identified, a Quality Action Team(QAT)
will be formed to complete a more intensive assessment utilizing a formal performance
improvement approach such as PDSA. The QAT involves all applicable departments and
represents all individuals involved in the process being assessed. Whether the action plan is
developed for a minor problem identified or developed by the Quality Action Team, the goal of
an action plan is to design new processes, improve or change work processes that do not meet the
company or customer's expectations, industry accepted standards of practice in order to improve
patient safety and reduce sentinel events.




D. Communication

Findings, conclusions, recommendations, and results will be disseminated to managers and staff
after each Performance Improvement meeting. Leadership is advised of all findings quarterly.

                                                8

===== PAGE 236 =====
Leadership will use results of Performance Improvement as a measure of the care and services
provided and to identify and define opportunities for improvement in order to meet the goals of
the mission statement.

When an improvement in the way the agency does business, provides care and services has been
accepted as a policy, practice or standard operating procedure, all applicable individuals will
receive communication and an implementation plan, from an appropriately designated
individual(s).

Process Improvements that are developed and or have been proven to be effective, efficient and
improve positive patient outcomes will be implemented company wide.

VII. Quality Action Team

In order to meet the goals of the mission statement, the Quality Action Team will meet as often
as needed. Quality Action Teams will include across-section of people who represent some part
of the process or problem being investigated.

The QAT members may be composed of a member from each aspect of our service.
1. Administrator
2. RN case manager
3. Billing/Support Staff
4. Human resource representative
5. Paraprofessionals

Team membership may include as little as 2 people or up to 10 participants. Most group
memberships will range from 2 to 5 individuals.

At times the organization may request participation in Quality Assurance from individuals
outside of the agency. These individuals may be physicians, referral sources or third party
payers. The assistance of these outside individuals helps in our quest to exceed customer
expectations for services, care, and safety. Persons not employed by the agency will be required
to sign a confidentiality statement/Business Associate Agreement. All patient information will
be absent of patient names and identifying data in accordance with HIPPA law.

VIII. Evaluation of the Plan

Annually or as often as needed, the Quality Assurance plan will be evaluated for effectiveness
and relationship to current needs. The summary of the findings will be presented to Leadership.

A.     Landlord is the owner of an office building located at 1049 Park Ave, Cranston, Rhode Island
02910 (the "Building");

B.      Tenant intends to operate a home nursing care provider in Cranston, Rhode Island subsequent
to its receipt of approval from the Rhode Island Department of Health, and the Health Services
Council, for a change in effective control related to Tenant's acquisition of Hope Nursing Care, LLC
as described in, and subject to, that certain Membership Interest Purchase Agreement("MIPA") by
and between Community Based Care, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company ("Buyer"), Tenant,
and Irene Aiyun Qi, an individual resident of Massachusetts ("Seller"); and,

Landlord is willing to lease the same to Tenant, all pursuant to the terms and conditions of this Lease.

which is hereby acknowledged, Landlord and Tenant hereby agree as follows:

business.

and legal costs incurred by Landlord.

         30.     Re-Entry by Landlord: No re-entry by Landlord or any action brought by Landlord to
 oust Tenant from the Premises shall operate to terminate this Lease unless Landlord shall give ten
(10) days prior written notice of termination to Tenant, in which event Tenant's liability shall be as
 above provided. No right or remedy granted to Landlord herein is intended to be exclusive of any
 other right or remedy, and cumulative and in addition to any other right or remedy hereunder or now
 or hereafter existing in law or equity or by statute. In the event of termination of this Lease, Tenant
 waives any and all rights to redeem the Premises either given by any stature now in effect or hereafter
 enacted.

        31.      Waiver of Rights: No waiver by Landlord or Tenant of any provision hereof shall be
 deemed to be a waiver of any other provision hereof or of any subsequent breach by Tenant of the
 same or any other provision. Landlord's consent to or approval of any act shall not be deemed to
 render unnecessary the obtaining of Landlord's consent to or approval of any subsequent act by
 Tenant. The acceptance of rent hereunder by Landlord shall not be a waiver of any preceding breach
 by Tenant of any provision hereof other than failure of Tenant to pay the particular rent so accepted
 regardless of Landlord's knowledge of said preceding breach at the time of acceptance of such rent.

        32.     Notices: Any notices required or permitted to be given under this Lease shall be in
 writing and shall be deemed to have been provided hereunder to a party (a) on the date of delivery
 when hand-delivered to a party,(b)on the date of receipt by the party as indicated on the return receipt
 when sent by United States registered or certified mail, return receipt requested, with proper postage
 prepaid, addressed to the party at their address listed below, or such other address set forth in any
 notice of change of address given in writing by the addressee to the addressor, or (c) on the date of
 receipt by the party as indicated by a facsimile confirmation report when sent by facsimile
 transmission to a facsimile number provided by the addressee.

        33.     Compliance with Laws:

              (a)      Tenant shall promptly execute and comply with all laws, ordinances, rules,
regulations and requirements of any or all federal, state and municipal authorities having jurisdiction
over the manner in which the Tenant's business is conducted, but only insofar as these laws,
ordinances, rules and regulations and requirements are violated by the conduct of Tenant's business.

               (b)       In the event that counsel for either party determines, pursuant to a written
opinion, that any one or more of the terms of this Lease (i) may reasonably be considered unlawful
or invalid under state or federal law, or (ii)jeopardizes or causes the loss of the federal or state tax-
exempt status of Tenant or any person controlling or controlled by it, the parties hereto agree to reform
this Lease (i) to eliminate the illegality or invalidity; (ii) to revise any provision which jeopardizes or
causes loss of the tax-exempt status, so that after the revision Tenant(or its controlling or controlled
person) will qualify or continue to qualify for tax-exempt status; and (iii) to eliminate or revise the
term or terms hereof in a manner to favorably resolve any enforcement action, threatened enforcement
action, audit or examination of any agency of state or local government. "Favorably resolve" means
that such matter will be resolved in such a manner that the agency of state or federal government
which is bringing or threatening the enforcement action, audit or examination does not conclude or
find that a term of this Lease (i) is unlawful or invalid under state or federal law, or (ii) would cause
the loss of the federal or state tax-exempt status of Tenant(or any person controlling or controlled by
it). In the event that there is a change in state or federal law that would render any one or more of the
terms of this Lease unlawful or invalid, or would jeopardize or cause the loss of the federal or state
tax-exempt status of Tenant or any person controlling or controlled by it, the parties hereto agree to
reform this Lease (i) to eliminate the illegality or invalidity to comply with such change or (ii) to
revise any provision which jeopardizes or causes the loss of the federal or state tax-exempt status of

Tenant or its controlling or controlled party, so that after the revision said party will qualify or
continue to qualify for tax-exempt status. The provisions of this Section shall survive the termination
of this Lease or the expiration, without renewal, of the term of this Lease.

        34.    Surrender: Upon the termination of this Lease or any extension or renewal hereof, the
Tenant shall surrender the Premises in good and clean condition and repair as when received,
excepting only normal wear and tear and damage by fire and other casualty damage covered by
insurance and paid to Landlord. Tenant shall not remain in the Premises without the benefit of a
written lease or renewal agreement executed by the parties hereto prior to the expiration of the then
existing term; notwithstanding the foregoing, if upon the expiration of this Lease, or any renewal
period hereof, Landlord and Tenant are engaged in good faith negotiations to amend and/or extend
the Lease terms, then Tenant shall be allowed to remain in the Premises during the duration of the
negotiations and, if such negotiations fail, for a reasonable period of time to allow Tenant's orderly
surrender of the Premises. The continued occupancy shall not be deemed as a 'holdover' or 'default'
of the terms of this agreement.

        35.     Liens: If Tenant shall cause any material to be furnished to the Premises or labor to
be performed thereon or therein, Landlord shall not under any circumstances be liable for the payment
of any expenses incurred or for the value of any work done or material furnished. All such work shall
be at Tenant's expense and Tenant shall be solely and wholly responsible to all contractors, laborers,
and material men furnishing labor and material to the Premises. Nothing herein shall authorize the
Tenant or any person dealing through, with or under Tenant to charge the Premises or any interest of
the Landlord therein or this Lease with any mechanic's liens or other lien or encumbrance whatever.

        36.    Benefits, Burdens and Entire Agreement: This Lease is binding on and benefits the
parties hereto and their respective heirs, legal representatives, successors, nominees and permitted
assigns. This Lease contains the entire agreement between the parties hereto with respect to the
Premises leased hereunder; further this Lease may not be modified, altered or amended, except by an
instrument in writing, executed by the parties hereto or their respective heirs, legal representatives,
successors, nominees or permitted assigns, and which instrument shall be attached hereto as an
amendment to this Lease and shall thereby become a part hereof.

       37.    Governing Law: This Lease shall be governed by and construed under the laws of the
State of Rhode Island, without regard to any conflict of laws principles therein.

        38.     Identification of Master List: Tenant and Landlord, or one or more entities related to
one of them, may have entered into other agreements unrelated to this Lease. All such agreements
are identified on that certain master list maintained and updated by Tenant(the "Master List"). Upon
written request to the CEO of Tenant, the Master List shall be made available for inspection by the
Secretary of Health and Human Services pursuant to Section 1877(e)(3)(A)(ii) of the Social Security
Act.

        The Member of CBC Rhode Island FT, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company ("Company"),
hereby enters into this "Agreement" effective as of September 29, 2021, and hereby agrees as follows:

         1.      Name; Formation; Term. The name of the Company is CBC Rhode Island FT, LLC.
Pursuant to the filing of the certificate of formation (the "Certificate") with the office of the Delaware
Secretary of State, the Company was formed on September 29, 2021, as a limited liability company in
accordance with the Delaware Limited Liability Company Act ("Act"). The existence of the Company
commenced on the date the Certificate was filed with the office of the Secretary of State of Delaware and
shall continue until the Company is dissolved pursuant to Section 10 of this Agreement.

         2.       Registered Office; Registered Agent; Principal Office; Other Offices. The registered office
of the Company required by the Act to be maintained in the State of Delaware is the registered office set
forth in the Certificate; provided that the Member may designate another office (which need not be a place
of business of the Company) in the manner provided by law. The registered agent of the Company in the
State of Delaware is the initial registered agent named in the Certificate; provided that the Member may

which need not be in the State of Delaware, and the Company shall maintain records there.

prohibited by the Act.

        4.      Member. The managing member of the Company is Community Based Care FT

distributions at such time, in such amounts and in such form as determined by the Member.

        7.       Indemnification; Exculpation.

                  (a)      The Company hereby agrees to indemnify, reimburse and hold harmless any
person(each an "Indemnified Person")to the fullest extent permitted under the Act, as the same now exists
or may hereafter be amended, substituted or replaced (but, in the case of any such amendment, substitution
or replacement only to the extent that such amendment, substitution or replacement permits the Company
to provide broader indemnification rights than the Company is providing immediately prior to such
amendment), against all expenses, liabilities and losses (including attorneys' fees,judgments, fines, excise
taxes or penalties) reasonably incurred or suffered by such person by reason of the fact that such person is
or was a member of the Company, is or was serving as an officer or authorized person of the Company or
is or was serving at the request ofthe Company as an officer, manager, director, principal, member,partner,
employee or agent of another legal entity, joint venture or other enterprise; provided, that, unless in
connection with such officer's appointment the Member specifies otherwise, no officer shall be indemnified
for any damages,judgments, penalties, fines, settlements or expenses (i)to the extent attributable to such
officer's gross negligence, willful misconduct, bad faith or violation of law, (ii) for any breach of any
fiduciary duty of such officer; (iii) for any breach of any representations, warranties or covenants by such
officer contained in any other agreement with the Company or its subsidiaries.or (iv) in any action brought
 by such officer, such officer's affiliates or the person of whom such officer is the legal representative.
Expenses, including attorneys' fees, incurred by any such Indemnified Person in defending a proceeding
 shall be paid by the Company in advance of the final disposition of such proceeding, including any appeal
 therefrom, upon receipt of an undertaking by or on behalfofsuch Indemnified Person to repay such amount
 if it shall ultimately be determined that such Indemnified Person is not entitled to be indemnified by the
 Company. The Company may, by action ofthe Member, provide indemnification to employees and agents
 ofthe Company with the same scope and effect as the foregoing indemnification of members, officers and
 authorized persons.

                                                Independent Auditors' Report


               The Board of Directors
               Community Based Care, LLC:

               Opinion

               We have audited the accompanying consolidated financial statements of Community Based Care,
               LLC and Subsidiaries, which comprise the consolidated balance sheets as of December 31, 2020
               and 2019, and the related consolidated statements of operations, members' equity and cash flows
               for the years then ended, and the related notes to the consolidated financial statements.

               In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements referred to above present fairly, in all
               material respects, the financial position of Community Based Care, LLC and Subsidiaries as of
               December 31, 2020 and 2019, and the results of their operations and their cash flows for the
               years then ended in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United
               States of America.

               Basis for Opinion

               We conducted our audits in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United
               States of America. Our responsibilities under those standards are further described in the
               A uditors' Responsibilities for the Audit of the Consolidated Financial Statements section of our
               report. We are required to be independent of Community Based Care, LLC and Subsidiaries and
               to meet our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with the relevant ethical requirements
               relating to our audits. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and
               appropriate to provide a basis for our audit opinion.

               Responsibilities of Management for the Consolidated Financial Statements

               Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of the consolidated financial
               statements in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of
               America, and for the design, implementation, and maintenance of internal control relevant to the
               preparation and fair presentation of consolidated financial statements that are free from material
               m isstatement, whether due to fraud or error.

                In preparing the consolidated financial statements, management is required to evaluate whether
                there are conditions or events, considered in the aggregate, that raise substantial doubt about
                Community Based Care, LLC and Subsidiaries' ability to continue as a going concern within one
                year after the date that the consolidated financial statements are available to be issued.




                                                               1

===== PAGE 277 =====
Auditors' Responsibilities for the Audit of the Consolidated Financial Statements

Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated financial
statements as a whole are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to
issue an auditors' report that includes our opinion. Reasonable assurance is a high level of
assurance but is not absolute assurance and therefore is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in
accordance with generally accepted auditing standards will always detect a material
misstatement when it exists. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from
fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery,
intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of internal control. Misstatements,
including omissions, are considered material if there is a substantial likelihood that, individually
or in the aggregate, they would influence the judgment made by a reasonable user based on the
consolidated financial statements.

In performing an audit in accordance with generally accepted auditing standards, we:

   • Exercise professional judgment and maintain professional skepticism throughout the
     audit.

   • Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the consolidated financial
     statements, whether due to fraud or error, and design and perform audit procedures
     responsive to those risks. Such procedures include examining, on a test basis, evidence
     regarding the amounts and disclosures in the consolidated financial statements.

   • Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant to the audit in order to design audit
     procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of
     expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of Community Based Care, LLC and
     Subsidiaries' internal control. Accordingly, no such opinion is expressed.

   • Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of
     significant accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluate the overall
     presentation of the consolidated financial statements.

   • Conclude whether, in our judgment, there are conditions or events, considered in the
     aggregate, that raise substantial doubt about Community Based Care, LLC and
     Subsidiaries' ability to continue as a going concern for a reasonable period of time.

We are required to communicate with those charged with governance regarding, among other
matters, the planned scope and timing of the audit, significant audit findings, and certain internal
control related matters that we identified during the audit.

Operations and Summary of Significant Accounting Policies

(a)     Operations

 Community Based Care, LLC ("CBC") is a Delaware Limited Liability Company
 established June 30, 2015, for the purpose of owning and holding the stock of companies
 that provide support services for individuals with intellectual and developmental
 disabilities. CBC provides leadership and support to its subsidiary holdings in their
 provision of quality services to individuals with disabilities. CBC purchased 100% of the
 stock of Homecare Management Corporation ("Homecare") effective August 14, 2015,
 100% of the stock of Hughes Behavioral and Mental Health Services, Inc. ("Hughes")
 effective September 18, 2015, 100% of the stock of Lindley Habilitation Services, Inc.
("Lindley") effective December 2, 2016, 100% of the stock of Community Innovations,
 Inc. ("CI") effective April 13, 2017, 100% of the stock of Suncoast New Options, Inc.
("Suncoast") effective June 30, 2018, 100% of the stock of Pinnacle Home Care, Inc.
("Pinnacle") effective July 31, 2019, and 100% of the stock of Quality Home Staffing,
 Inc. ("QHS") effective July 31, 2020. Homecare, Hughes, Lindley, Pinnacle and QHS
 provide community based services under the North Carolina Innovations Medicaid
 Waiver program and services funded by the State of North Carolina. In addition to these
 services, CI provides services to individuals with intellectual and developmental
 disabilities in ICF-IID and DDA group home settings. Suncoast provides in home and
 community based I/DD services under the Florida Medicaid Waiver program. These
 respective companies will be collectively referred to as "Company". The Company's
 corporate offices are located in Hickory, North Carolina.

(b)     Principles of Consolidation

 The consolidated financial statements include all of the accounts of CBC, Homecare,
 Hughes, Lindley, CI, Suncoast, Pinnacle and QHS. All material intercompany accounts
 and transactions have been eliminated.

(c)     Cash and Restricted Cash

 For purposes of reporting cash flows, cash and restricted cash include cash on hand, cash
 in banks, consumers' personal funds, and highly liquid investments with an original
 maturity date of three months or less.

 The Company maintains its cash in bank deposit accounts which, at times, may exceed
 federally insured limits. The Company has not experienced any losses in such accounts.
 The Company believes it is not exposed to any significant credit risk on cash.




                                          9

===== PAGE 285 =====
         COMMUNITY BASED CARE,LLC AND SUBSIDIARIES

                  Notes to Consolidated Financial Statements

                         December 31, 2020 and 2019


Operations and Summary of Significant Accounting Policies, Continued

(d)    Property and Equipment

Property and equipment is recorded at cost if purchased. The Company capitalizes
property and equipment if its value is greater than $500 and its useful life is more than
one year. Depreciation of property and equipment is computed by the straight-line
method over the estimated useful lives of the respective assets. The estimated useful
lives range from 2 to 10 years for property and equipment. Depreciation expense
amounted to $689,871 and $631,422 for 2020 and 2019, respectively.

(e)    Revenue and Accounts Receivable

The Company provides services to individuals under the North Carolina and Florida
Medicaid programs. The Company derived substantially all of its revenue from services
provided to clients under these programs for the years ended December 31, 2020 and
2019. The North Carolina Medicaid Program provides for reimbursement of allowable
expenses related to the care of clients, up to a maximum amount. The Florida Medicaid
Program provides for reimbursement of services pre-authorized by the Agency for
Persons with Disabilities(APD).

Revenues are recognized as services are provided to consumers in an amount that reflects
the consideration the Company expects to be entitled to in exchange for those services
and a related receivable is recorded. Management evaluates receivable balances
periodically. Based upon these reviews, management will establish an allowance for
uncollectible accounts or will write-off specific amounts if deemed uncollectible. In this
review, management considers historical losses, results of any ongoing collection efforts,
and any other relevant information available. Substantially all of the Company's revenue
was derived from performance obligations satisfied at a point in time for 2020 and 2019.

(f)    Goodwill

The Company amortizes goodwill on the straight-line method over ten years unless a
shorter useful life can be demonstrated and evaluates goodwill for impairment at the
entity level when a triggering event occurs. During the years ended December 31, 2020
and 2019, no triggering events occurred and thus, no impairment testing was performed.
The amortization period assigned to goodwill associated with all of the various
acquisitions from 2015 through 2020 is ten years. Amortization for the years ended
December 31, 2020 and 2019, was $3,403,058 and $3,015,214, respectively.




                                          10

===== PAGE 286 =====
         COMMUNITY BASED CARE,LLC AND SUBSIDIARIES

           Notes to Consolidated Financial Statements, Continued




Operations and Summary of Significant Accounting Policies, Continued

(g)    Intangible Assets

Intangible assets consist of client lists obtained from companies that provided similar
services as the Company. Intangible assets are amortized using the straight-line method
over ten years. Amortization for the years ended December 31, 2020 and 2019, was
$58,385 and $46,700, respectively.

(h)    Income Taxes

CBC has elected to be taxed as a partnership for Federal and state tax purposes.
Accordingly, no provision has been made for Federal and state income taxes because the
taxes are the responsibility of the individual members. Homecare, Hughes, Lindley, CI,
Suncoast, Pinnacle and QHS are C corporations.

Deferred taxes are provided on a liability method whereby deferred tax assets are
recognized for deductible temporary differences and operating loss and tax credit
carryforwards and deferred tax liabilities are recognized for taxable temporary
differences. Temporary differences are the differences between the reported amounts of
assets and liabilities and their tax bases. Deferred tax assets are reduced by a valuation
allowance when, in the opinion of management, it is more likely than not that some
portion or all of the deferred tax assets will not be realized. Deferred tax assets and
liabilities are adjusted for the effects of changes in tax laws and rates on the date of
enactment.

The Company has implemented the accounting guidance for uncertainty in income taxes
using the provisions of Financial Accounting Standards Board (FASB) ASC 740,Income
Taxes. Using that guidance, tax positions initially need to be recognized in the financial
statements when it is more-likely-than-not the positions will be sustained upon
examination by the tax authorities. Such tax positions initially and subsequently need to
be measured as the largest amount of tax benefit that has a greater than 50% likelihood of
being realized upon ultimate settlement with the tax authority assuming full knowledge of
the positions and relevant facts. The Company believes that it has appropriate support for
the income tax positions taken and to be taken in its tax returns and that its accruals for
tax liabilities are adequate for all open tax years based on an assessment of many factors
including experience and interpretations of tax laws applied to the facts of each matter.
The Company recognizes interest accrued related to unrecognized tax benefits in interest
expense and penalties in operating expenses. No such interest and penalties were
recognized during 2020 and 2019.

(1)   Operations and Summary of Significant Accounting Policies, Continued

      (i)     Advertising Costs

      Advertising costs are charged to operations when incurred. Advertising expense was
      $56,290 and $84,363 for 2020 and 2019, respectively.

      (j)     Fair Value Measurements

      The Company uses fair value measurements to record fair value adjustments to certain
      liabilities and to determine fair value disclosures of derivative financial instruments on a
      recurring basis.

      The provisions of Financial Accounting Standards Board (FASB) ASC 820, Fair Value
      Measurements, define fair value, establishes a consistent framework for measuring fair
      value and expands disclosure requirements for fair value measurements. ASC 820, Fair
       Value Measurements, establishes a fair value hierarchy that prioritizes the inputs to
      valuation techniques used to measure fair value. The hierarchy gives the highest priority
      to unadjusted quoted prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities (Level 1
      measurements) and the lowest priority to measurements involving significant
      unobservable inputs (Level 3 measurements). The three levels of the fair value hierarchy
      are as follows:

      Level 1 measurements are quoted prices (unadjusted) in active markets for identical
      assets or liabilities that the Company has the ability to access at the measurement date.

      Level 2 measurements are inputs other than quoted prices included within Level I that
      are observable for the asset or liability, either directly or indirectly.

      Level 3 measurements are unobservable inputs for the asset or liability.

      (k)     Interest Rate Swap Agreements

       The Company holds derivative financial instruments for the purpose of hedging the risks
       of certain identifiable and anticipated transactions. In general, the types of risks hedged
       are those relating to the variability of future cash flows caused by movements in interest
       rates. In hedging the transactions, the Company, in the normal course of business, holds
       interest rate swap agreements that hedge the cash flows of variable-rate long-term debt.
       The Company's derivative agreements are entered into only for the purpose of hedging
       such risks, not for speculation. Generally, the Company enters into hedging relationships
       such that changes in the cash flows of transactions being hedged are expected to be offset
       by corresponding changes in the value of the derivative. The Company's hedging
       relationships existed as described in note 8 of the financial statements.

       revenue and expenses during the periods reported. Estimates are used when accounting
       for allowance for doubtful accounts, depreciation and contingencies, among others.
       Actual results could differ from these estimates.

(4)     Deferred Revenue

In 2020, the Company received $1,837,798 of CARES Act Provider Relief Funds ("PRF") from
the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services ("HHS"). The funds, established by the
CARES Act, are for Medicaid fee-for-service providers to provide financial relief during the
Coronavirus pandemic. Under the terms of the act, the funds are to reimburse Medicaid
providers for healthcare related expenses and decreases in year-over-year consumer care revenue
attributable to Coronavirus through June 30, 2021. Provider relief payments not fully expended
on healthcare related expenses and/or attributed to decreases in net consumer revenue may be
subject to recoupment in 2021. No assurance can be provided that the Company will retain the
full amount of the funds. However, the Company recognized $1,452,302 in other operating
revenue during 2020 as the result of healthcare related expenses and decreases in net consumer
revenue. Deferred revenue at December 31, 2020, represents the remaining proceeds of
$385,495 which are recorded as conditional and not recognized as revenue until the conditions
are substantially met or explicitly waived.


(5)     Due to Previous Owners

Due to previous owners at December 31, 2020, represents amounts withheld from the original
purchase prices of QHS, Pinnacle and CI until the final adjustments to the purchase prices were
made. The amounts are expected to be settled in 2021.

Due to previous owners at December 31, 2019, represents amounts withheld from the original
purchase prices of Pinnacle, Suncoast and CI until the final adjustments to the purchase prices
were made. The amount due to Suncoast and a portion of the amount due to CI was settled in
2020.

(8)     Interest Rate Swap Agreements

Accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America establishes accounting
and reporting standards for derivative financial instruments, including certain derivative
instruments embedded in other host contracts (collectively referred to as embedded derivatives)
and for hedging activities. The standards require an entity to recognize all derivatives as either
assets or liabilities in the balance sheet and measure those instruments at fair value.

The Company is exposed to various market risks, primarily related to adverse fluctuations in the
interest rates. The Company's risk management policy permits the use of certain interest rate
swaps as cash flow hedges of interest rate risk. The Company's policy does not permit the use of
interest rate swaps for trading or speculative purposes. All interest rate swaps entered into by the
Company must be designated and documented as cash flow hedges at inception. The
effectiveness of such designated hedging relationships is tested and documented in accordance
with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.

                                                  20

===== PAGE 296 =====
                 COMMUNITY BASED CARE,LLC AND SUBSIDIARIES

                   Notes to Consolidated Financial Statements, Continued




(8)    Interest Rate Swap Agreements, Continued

The effective portion of each interest rate swap's gain or loss is initially reported as a component
of accumulated other comprehensive income and is subsequently reclassified into earnings when
the hedged exposure affects earnings. The ineffective portion, if any, of each interest rate swap's
gain or loss is reported currently in earnings.

At December 31, 2020 and 2019, the Company had an interest rate swap to effectively convert
$4,437,500 and $4,750,000, respectively of variable-rate borrowings under the Company's long-
term debt to a fixed-rate basis beginning July 2018. The interest rate swap is considered a level
3 measurement. As of December 31, 2020 and 2019, the interest rate swap was a designated and
documented cash flow hedge of the risk of changes in the cash flows attributable to fluctuations
in the variable benchmark interest rate associated with $4,437,500 and $4,750,000, respectively
of outstanding borrowings under one of the Company's term loans. The interest rate swap was
effective at the balance sheet date. At December 31, 2020 and 2019, the fair value of the interest
rate swap was a liability of $285,349 and $211,413, respectively.

At December 31, 2020 and 2019, the Company had an interest rate swap to effectively convert
$4,437,500 and $4,812,500, respectively of variable-rate borrowings under the Company's long-
term debt to a fixed-rate basis beginning April 2019. The interest rate swap is considered a level
3 measurement. As of December 31, 2020 and 2019, the interest rate swap was a designated and
documented cash flow hedge of the risk of changes in the cash flows attributable to fluctuations
in the variable benchmark interest rate associated with $4,437,500 and $4,812,500, respectively
of outstanding borrowings under two of the Company's term loans. The interest rate swap was
effective at the balance sheet date. At December 31, 2020 and 2019, the fair value of the interest
rate swap was a liability of $228,735 and $129,843, respectively.

At December 31, 2019, the Company had an interest rate swap to effectively convert $7,818,750
of variable-rate borrowings under the Company's long-term debt to a fixed-rate basis beginning
April 2017. The interest rate swap is considered a level 3 measurement. As of December 31,
2019, the interest rate swap was a designated and documented cash flow hedge of the risk of
changes in the cash flows attributable to fluctuations in the variable benchmark interest rate
associated with $7,818,750 of outstanding borrowings under one of the Company's term loans.
The interest rate swap was effective at the balance sheet date. At December 31, 2019, the fair
value of the interest rate swap was a liability of $827.

During the years ended December 31, 2020 and 2019, the Company had no material
ineffectiveness or other gain or loss excluded from the assessment of hedge effectiveness
reported in the consolidated statement of operations but did reclassify approximately $200,200
and $3,900 into interest expense related to interest rate swap payments on the loans.

(9)    Equity Incentive Plan

Effective June 15, 2016, the Company adopted the Community Based Care, LLC 2016 Equity
Incentive Plan ("Plan"). Under the Plan, the Company has the authority to grant equity incentive
unit awards ("Class M units") to certain employees and directors, based on criteria established by
the Plan. The number of units subject to awards under the Plan may not exceed 2,868,192 units.

The Company issued 1,103,039 Class M units on June 15, 2016, 254,547 Class M units on
January 1, 2017, 809,925 Class M units on January 1, 2018, 173,556 Class M units on January 1,
2019, 360,000 Class M units on April 1, 2019, and 221,974 Class M units on January 1, 2020, to
several key employees and directors. The Company canceled 84,849 Class M units in 2018
awarded to a former employee. These remaining Class M units vest over 4 years, with 1,703,639
units based on a time vesting schedule and 1,134,553 units based on achievement of targeted
performance goals. The time vesting units vest at the rate of 25% to 50% annually, depending
on the individual, beginning retroactively as of September 30, 2015, for the units issued in 2016
and ending December 31, 2023. The performance based units will vest in full upon the
achievement by the Company of a targeted EBITDA amount for any single fiscal year. The
targeted EBITDA amount is specific to each individual issuance and may vary between
issuances.

The Company has recorded compensation expense of $521,470 and $913,165 for the years ended
December 31, 2020 and 2019, respectively based on the estimated market value of the unit
awards on the respective grant dates. Compensation expense of $521,470 and $366,644 for 2020
and 2019, respectively relates to 254,767 units and 270,461 units that vested in the years ended
December 31, 2020 and 2019, respectively based on a time vesting schedule. Compensation
expense of $546,521 for 2019 relates to 574,901 units that vested based on performance.

(11)    Accounting Impact of COVID-19

On January 30, 2020, the World Health Organization ("WHO") announced a global health
emergency because of a new strain of coronavirus originating in Wuhan, China (the "COVID-19
outbreak") and the risks to the international community as the virus spreads globally beyond its
point of origin. In March 2020, the WHO classified the COVID-19 outbreak as a pandemic,
based on the rapid increase in exposure globally.




                                                 23

                  Notes to Consolidated Financial Statements, Continued




(11)   Accounting Impact of COVID-19, Continued

The full impact of the COVID-19 outbreak continues to evolve as of the date of this report. As
such, it is uncertain as to the full magnitude that the pandemic will have on the Company's
financial condition, liquidity, and future results of operations. Management is actively
monitoring the global situation on its financial condition, liquidity, operations, suppliers,
industry, and workforce. Given the daily evolution of the COVID-19 outbreak and the global
responses to curb its spread, the Company is not able to estimate the effects of the COVID-19
outbreak on its results of operations, financial condition, or liquidity for the year ending
December 31, 2021.

Although the Company cannot estimate the length or gravity of the impact of the COVID-19
outbreak at this time, if the pandemic continues, it may have an adverse effect on the Company's
results of future operations, financial position, and liquidity in 2021.


(12)   Subsequent Events

The Company has evaluated subsequent events through March 29, 2020, the date which the
consolidated financial statements were available to be issued. There were no significant
subsequent events identified by management for disclosure in the consolidated financial
statements.

The Board of Directors
Community Based Care, LLC:

We have audited the accompanying consolidated financial statements of Community Based Care, LLC and
Subsidiaries, which comprise the consolidated balance sheets as of December 31, 2019 and 2018, and the related
consolidated statements of operations, members' equity and cash flows for the years then ended, and the related
notes to the consolidated financial statements.

Management's Responsibility for the Consolidated Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of these consolidated financial statements in
accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America; this includes the design,
implementation, and maintenance of internal control relevant to the preparation and fair presentation of financial
statements that are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.

Auditor's Responsibility

Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these consolidated financial statements based on our audits. We
conducted our audits in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United States of America.
Those standards require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the
consolidated financial statements are free from material misstatement.

An audit involves performing procedures to obtain evidence about the amounts and disclosures in the consolidated
financial statements. The procedures selected depend on the auditor's judgment, including the assessment of the
risks of material misstatement of the consolidated financial statements, whether due to fraud or error. In making
those risk assessments, the auditor considers internal control relevant to the entity's preparation and fair presentation
of the consolidated financial statements in order to design audit procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances,
but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the entity's internal control. Accordingly,
we express no such opinion. An audit also includes evaluating the appropriateness of accounting policies used and
the reasonableness of significant accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall
presentation of the consolidated financial statements.

We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our audit
opinion.

Opinion

In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements referred to above present fairly, in all material respects, the
financial position of Community Based Care, LLC and Subsidiaries as of December 31, 2019 and 2018, and the
results of their operations and their cash flows for the years then ended in accordance with accounting principles
generally accepted in the United States of America.
